| L15-E386 | **Adopted: an up-counter from zero that only the exit test uses, merged into the index it steps beside** (AGXNarrowInductions mergeUpCounter, -agx-merge-up-counters). An MLIR strided loop `scf.for %j = %lane to %n step 32` narrows to a counter c = 0, 32, ... against the trip t = min(n - lane, 1024) and an index i = row * 1024 + lane + c for the address, LSR stepping each with an iadd. With c' = c + s used only by the latch's c' <u t (or c' != t) and i' = i + s by the same s, i' = i0 + c' mod 2^32, so the test becomes i' != i0 + t (equality: exact), i' <u i0 + t where upperBound(i0) + bound(t) + s < 2^32, and otherwise, s a power of two and bound(t) + s < 2^32, i' != i0 + ((max(t, 1) + s - 1) & -s), the first multiple of s c' reaches (the row of an unbounded threadgroup position: block_id's MLIR upper_bound is not in the IR). The bound is the smaller of upperBound (now also through truncations and unsigned minimums) and scalar evolution's unsigned range. Exhaustive 8-bit model of the rounded exit over every start, trip and step 1-32 (16.2M iterations): 0 mismatches. agx_run, HEAD vs E386, softmax_rows (19 n from 0 to 2^63 + 5, two grids), stride_loop (16 n incl. negative), gemm_smem_tile (10 kdim): 64/64 bit-identical. MLIR bench tput: softmax_rows 209.9 -> 205.6 (-2.0%, three loops each one iadd and a register fewer), stride_loop 18.7 -> 18.2 (-2.7%), gemm_smem_tile 111.7 -> 107.8 (-3.5%, 19 -> 18 GPRs, 80 -> 88 groups; one group's cycles +0.05% for the preheader's rounding); agx-bench unchanged at --tolerance 0. Test: merge-up-counter.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E387 | **Adopted: E270's e^(x - m) without overflow tests also in an in-place pass** (AGXCodeGenPrepare expBelowLoopMax, -agx-exp-in-place-stores). E270 required that nothing between the maximum's loop and x's load write memory, which left softmax_rows's third pass (x[j] = e^(x[j] - m) / s in place) with both tests. Now a store in x's loop is allowed where its address is x's load's (one SCEV), the load dominates it (each iteration reads before it writes) and no two iterations' accesses overlap (disjointIterations: the address is invariant terms plus c * ext(r) with c at least the access size and c * 2^width(r) within the address's width, or ext(r) with r's step at least the size, r an affine recurrence of the loop with step * maximum backedge-taken count below 2^width(r)): every x is still the value the first loop's iteration took in (another thread's write would be a race). agx_run, E386 vs E387, softmax_rows over normal, x60, x1000, 5% special values (+-inf, NaN, +-3e38, +-denormals, +-0, 88.7, 89, -87, -104, 1e30), whole rows of -inf / inf / NaN / 3e38 / denormals / -3e38, and random bit patterns, 9 n from 1 to 5000, two grids: 108/108 bit-identical. MLIR bench softmax_rows 143 -> 139 instructions, cycles 3100.5 -> 3062.3, tput 205.6 -> 194.1 (-5.6%); agx-bench unchanged at --tolerance 0. Test: exp-in-place.ll (a store to the next element or a 2-byte step keep the tests). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E388 | **Adopted: NaN tests of values no execution makes a NaN folded** (AGXCodeGenPrepare foldNeverNaNTests, NeverNaN, -agx-never-nan-tests). The PTX front end's max.f32 is maximumnum then select(uno m, default NaN, m); InstCombine turns a maximumnum of that into select(uno a, b, maximumnum(a, b)). A running maximum from a number (-3e38) is never a NaN: maximumnum gives one only where both operands are. Value tracking cannot see it through the loop's phi; NeverNaN assumes a phi while it checks its incoming values (coinductively: every value a phi takes was computed earlier, so if no value of the assumed phis is a NaN where theirs are not, none ever is), with number constants, maximumnum / minimumnum of one never-NaN operand, canonicalize / fneg / fabs / copysign, a select on a NaN test of never-NaN values by its picked arm, other selects and phis by all values, and value tracking for the rest; proven values are cached until an assumption fails (attn_decode's unrolled chain of eight). fcmp uno / ord of never-NaN operands become false / true and their selects the arm. agx-bench: attn_decode 2622 -> 2560 bytes, 37 -> 36 GPRs, cycles 26612.7 -> 26337.2, tput 2323.0 -> 2278.6 (-1.9%); softmax_online 1498 -> 1438 bytes, cycles 2524.6 -> 2504.3, tput 163.8 -> 161.3 (-1.5%; the tests left are of values reloaded from threadgroup memory); nothing else changed. agx_run, E387 vs E388: softmax_online (normal, 3% +-inf / NaN / +-0 / +-3e38 / denormals, a row of NaN, rows of -3e38 and -inf, random bit patterns; n 1 to 2048; 64, 96 and 256 threads) and attn_decode (normal, special f16 values in q / K / V, K x300, a NaN q head; seq 1 to 512; scale 0.088 and 1e30): 115/115 bit-identical. Test: never-nan-tests.ll (a maximum from an argument, or of two loads, keeps its test). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E389 | **Adopted: a float min / max nsz where nothing its value reaches depends on the sign of a zero** (AGXCodeGenPrepare zeroSignFreeMinMax, ZeroSignFree, -agx-zero-sign-free-minmax). The lowering orders -0 below +0 with an and / or of the operands' bits, its flush and a select on their equality (three of a maximumnum's four instructions; E264 left them). Without the fix the result differs at most in a zero's sign (or a denormal its flush makes that zero). Allowed uses: compares, exponentials (e^+-0 = 1), fabs, a copysign's magnitude, and, passing the difference on, phis, select arms, fneg / fadd / fsub / fmul / fma / canonicalize and min / max (each then differs at most in its own zero's sign), with phis and selects assumed while their uses are checked; a store, a division, a bitcast or anything else keeps the fix. Fires on attn_decode's threadgroup maximum (eight a loop iteration, which only offsets exp2(x - m)) and softmax_online's second butterfly. agx-bench: attn_decode 2560 -> 2380 bytes, cycles 26337.2 -> 26319.6, tput 2278.6 -> 2276.2 (-0.1%); softmax_online 1438 -> 1418 bytes, cycles 2504.3 -> 2498.9, tput 161.3 -> 160.2 (-0.7%); nothing else changed. agx_run, E388 vs E389, E388's 115 inputs (NaN / +-inf / +-0 / denormals): 115/115 bit-identical. Test: zero-sign-free-minmax.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E390 | **Rejected: device loads hoisted in a region E239 keeps in its original order** (prototype, AGXScheduleDAGMILive: on a revert, the original order with ready device loads picked first, kept where its register halves cost neither threads nor SIMD-groups on a core, -agx-sched-hoist-device-loads). gemm_smem_tile's loop is one region (barriers do not split it); the generic order doubled its registers (26 -> 72 halves) and was reverted, which also keeps the second tile load behind the first one's wait and threadgroup store. Hoisted, both loads issue together at the same 26 halves (18 GPRs): one group's cycles gemm_smem_tile 5257.8 -> 5076.5 (-3.4%), matrixMul 5262.3 -> 5081.8 (-3.4%), but tput 107.8 -> 109.4 (+1.5%) and 116.9 -> 118.1 (+1.0%): the 88 / 72 resident groups already hide the first load's latency, and issuing the two together costs throughput in agx_cost. Nothing else changed. Reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E391 | **Adopted: MLIR's upper_bounds of thread and threadgroup positions carried to the AGX backend's loop analyses** (GPUToAIR: "agx-upper-bounds:<AIR system value>"="x,y,z" kernel attributes, 0 where unknown; AGXSpecialRegisterRanges upperBounds / AGX::knownUpperBound, -agx-upper-bound-ranges; AGXNarrowInductions upperBound also honours a call's range). AIR's position vectors carry a range only where all three components are bounded, so softmax_rows's block_id x upper_bound 256 and gemm_smem_tile's global_id x / y upper_bound 1024 were lost. Kept: the bounds of reads in the kernel's entry block before any op with regions or a call (which every execution reaches, so a bound the launch breaks is undefined behaviour for the whole kernel; gpu.block_id / global_id / thread_id say >= upper_bound is UB). Used by AGXNarrowInductions (E386's exit then <u i0 + n, without the rounding; address offsets): MLIR bench softmax_rows 139 -> 127 instructions, 23 -> 22 GPRs, tput 194.1 -> 190.4 (-1.9%); gemm_smem_tile 89 -> 85 instructions, tput 107.8 -> 106.5 (-1.2%); nothing else changed (agx-bench kernels have no such attributes). Measured and not adopted: the bounds also as the reads' range attributes for instruction selection (-agx-upper-bound-ranges=2): gemm_simd_matrix 88.9 -> 85.6 (-3.7%) but layernorm_rows 46.1 -> 49.5 (+7.4%, 19 -> 21 GPRs), transpose_smem 17.3 -> 18.3 (+5.8%), saxpy_vec4 48.7 -> 50.1 (+2.9%) (E191's address folds). agx_run, E389 vs E391: softmax_rows (19 n, two grids) and gemm_smem_tile (10 kdim), 48/48 bit-identical. Tests: gpu-to-air.mlir (upper_bounds), merge-up-counter.ll (bounded_grid). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E392 | **Measured, not adopted: more constant offsets per address as 32-bit indices** (-agx-offset-index-max, default 1: performOffsetIndexCombine's (b + (zext(x) << s)) + c as b + zext(x + c') for at most that many offsets of one sum). max 3: MLIR bench conv3x3 tput 14.9 -> 13.8 (-7.4%) but one group's cycles 250.7 -> 370.0 (+48%: its 64-bit base and two immediate-offset loads become index loads after 32-bit adds of 258 and 516, the first of them now waiting on the imadd), layernorm_rows tput 46.1 -> 45.5 (-1.3%, cycles +3.6%), silu_f16x4 83.3 -> 83.8 (+0.6%), agx-bench attn_decode 2276.2 -> 2269.7 (-0.3%); max 2: conv3x3 and silu_f16x4 alone. With E391's bounds as range attributes as well (-agx-upper-bound-ranges=2): gemm_simd_matrix -3.7%, conv3x3 -7.4%, silu_f16x4 -1.0%, but layernorm_rows +2.8%, saxpy_vec4 +2.9%, transpose_smem +3.5%. Throughput gains that lengthen one group's latency this much, and a regression either way, leave the default. | measured | -- |
| L15-E393 | **Adopted (gate): agx-loop-fuzz.py, a differential fuzz of loop induction code** (llvm/utils/agx-stress; agx-stress.py runs it as fuzz-loop, so eight fuzzers). Kernels of one to three strided loops in the shape MLIR's scf.for from a lane lowers to: a counter from zero by s (i32, or i64 against a zero-extended trip) beside an index start + c that a masked load and a running hash use; the trip a bounded umin of an input, exit c' <u t or, for a multiple of s, c' != t; guarded do-while or plain while loops; starts from thread positions (bounded), shifted / row-and-lane / negated ones and input words that wrap; steps 1-128, powers of two and not; sometimes the counter in the hash too; and, in 40%, a running maximumnum with the PTX front end's default-NaN select, from -3e38 or an input word, whose bits are stored (inputs without denormals, 3% +-0 / +-inf / NaNs). Every other seed through opt -O2 first; 1024 threads, 40% boundary inputs, every word against a Python model; an agx_run failure or a 60 s timeout (a wrong exit test) is a failure. E386 changes the code of 60 of 200 kernels. Sabotage checks: E386 without its rounding (i' != i0 + n) fails 8 of 40 seeds (agx_run runs away); NeverNaN taking every phi as never a NaN fails 29 of 200 (a NaN start's payload stored). The real compiler: 600 + 200 seeds, 0 failing. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E394 | **Open lead (not done): conv3x3's interior loop steps six 64-bit tap pointers** (agx-bench cuda_conv3x3, its versioned in-bounds copy "81.inside": nine taps at inttoptr(K_j + P), P the channel offset stepped by H W 4 bytes, six invariant bases K_j = in + 4 (sext(row_j) + sext(x)) - 4 and the like). LSR keeps a pointer pair per base: six 64-bit adds and twelve registers an iteration. One stepped base K_0 + P and the taps as 32-bit index registers (K_j - K_0) / 4 would be one 64-bit add and six registers, but the index is signed 32-bit and the differences are of sexts of separately computed i32 row values (the PTX front end's int arithmetic carries no nsw), so |K_j - K_0| / 4 < 2^31 is not provable from the IR; a runtime test of the six differences in the preheader (a second version of the loop) would be. Also seen, no change: permute4d's twelve 64-bit divisions already take the 32-bit bypass (R8, E221); index_add's index loads cannot pass the float atomics they may alias. | open | -- |
| L15-E395 | **Adopted: E394's lead, a loop's taps at inttoptr(k_j + p) addressed from one base in a copy that runs where their offsets fit** (AGXAlignmentVersioning versionTapBases, -agx-tap-bases-min, 5). In a single-block innermost loop (versionGuards' copies), device accesses at inttoptr(k_j + p) or a constant-offset GEP of one, p stepped by the loop and at least 5 distinct invariant k_j, all of one element size e: a copy of the loop addresses them from inttoptr(k_0 + p) and gep iN e, base, sext(i32 (k_j - k_0 + c) / e) (the constant c into the index; on k_0 itself an immediate), entered where every k_j - k_0 + c is a multiple of e whose quotient fits 32 signed bits, for the whole SIMD-group (ballot), the loop as it was elsewhere; the dead inttoptrs and their additions are deleted (loop strength reduction would keep a pointer for each). A hand edit of the IR before loop strength reduction first: conv3x3 tput 1222.9 -> 921.0. agx-bench conv3x3: six stepped 64-bit pointers -> one, cycles 16389.1 -> 15224.5 (-7.1%), tput 1222.9 -> 899.2 (-26.5%), 55 -> 63 GPRs (the same 24 groups), 1728 -> 2478 bytes, waits 13 -> 17; nothing else changed (MLIR bench unchanged). With a minimum of 3 or 4 bases it also fired on layernorm_f16 (4 bases: tput +26.8%), rmsnorm and swiglu (3: +31.8%, +64.9%), whose accesses loop strength reduction already served from fewer registers. agx_run conv3x3, E391 vs E395, nine shapes (C x H x W x K 8x56x56x2, 3x20x37x2, 4x16x16x1, 1x1x1x1, 1x3x3x2, 64x56x56x1, 5x17x33x3, 2x2x40x1, 7x40x2x2) against NumPy: bit-identical, and so with the check forced false (the fallback path). Test: tap-bases.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E396 | **Adopted: E395 not where p is an extended 32-bit index, and then from 3 bases** (versionTapBases: p = ext(x), ext(x) << c or ext(x) * c skipped; -agx-tap-bases-min 5 -> 3). E395's regressions at 3-4 bases (rmsnorm, swiglu, layernorm_f16) were loops whose p is sext(i32 index) << 2: loop strength reduction already gives their accesses one 32-bit index register from uniform or invariant bases, and the copy turned that into a stepped 64-bit pointer. With them skipped, minimums of 3 and 4 change nothing in agx-bench and the MLIR bench against E395 (conv3x3's p, a 64-bit channel offset, still qualifies); 3 keeps the rule general. Test: tap-bases.ll (@index). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E397 | **Rejected: a loop's 64-bit integer addresses recomputed from its 32-bit counter in a copy that runs where the counter cannot wrap** (AGXAlignmentVersioning prototype, versionCounterAddresses, -agx-counter-addresses-min 2). A PTX `for (int k = k0; k < n; k += s) ... a[k] ... b[k]` arrives with opt's 64-bit address recurrences p_j = p_j0, + e_j s beside the i32 k (a 64-bit add and a register pair each). With b_j = p_j0 - e_j sext(k0), p_j = b_j + e_j sext(k) wherever k has not wrapped, which k0 <= INT_MAX - s and n <= INT_MAX - s + 1 ensure (each iteration's k < n): a copy computing the p_j so, entered on those tests for the whole SIMD-group (ballot). A hand edit of spmv_csr_vector's IR before loop strength reduction (its uniform kernel-argument bases and k as the index, no copy) gives 11 -> 9 loop instructions, 12 -> 8 GPRs, tput 32.7 -> 22.8, but that needs k's no-wrap, which the PTX front end's int arithmetic does not carry (no nsw). The versioned prototype: spmv_csr_vector tput 32.7 -> 37.7, 12 -> 17 GPRs, cycles 447.9 -> 506.2 (two iterations a lane: the tests, ballots and the bases computed per thread cost more than the adds saved, and both copies' registers count); q8_matvec 206.3 -> 227.1; q4_matvec 293.3 -> 296.5. Reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E398 | **Adopted: a loop's access at B[zext(x + y)] beside one indexed by y rebased to (B + zext(x))[zext(y)]** (AGXCodeGenPrepare rebaseLoopIndices, -agx-rebase-loop-indices; AGX::scevUpperBound exports AGXNarrowInductions' bound). New MLIR bench kernel fir_taps (an FIR filter / 1-D convolution with a runtime tap count, y[i] = sum_{k < K} w[k] x[i + k], K = 9): loop strength reduction kept a counter for i + k and one for k, an add each an iteration. Where x is invariant, y varies, x + y cannot wrap (upperBound: the thread position's E391 bound 65536 and MLIR's min(K, 64) trip) and another access of the loop has zext(y) as its index SCEV, the access is at (B + zext(x))[zext(y)]: one counter, x's base computed once (a 64-bit add before the loop). Not for a constant x (E250's shared base with immediate indices, which loop-index-bases.ll caught). fir_taps loop 7 -> 6 instructions, tput 29.5 -> 28.2 (-4.4%); nothing else changed in agx-bench or the MLIR bench. agx_run fir_taps, E397 vs E398, K = 0, 1, 2, 9, 63, 64, 65, 1000, 2^40 on 1024 and 65536 threads: 18/18 bit-identical, within 1e-5 of NumPy. Test: rebase-loop-indices.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E399 | **Adopted: relu's portable zero select dropped** (AGXCodeGenPrepare reluZeroSelects, -agx-relu-zero-selects). AIR has no maximum, so MLIR's arith.maximumf(x, 0) reaches the backend as maxnum(x, +0), select(x == 0, +0, ...) for -0 (maxnum may give either zero) and the NaN select. maxnum(x, +0) as select(freeze x > 0, freeze x, +0) (one fcmpsel, as it is lowered anyway) is +0 wherever x == 0, and so is minnum of it with a constant above zero (relu6), so the zero select's arms agree: it goes. alive2: select(x == 0, +0, maxnum(x, +0)) and with minnum(.., 6) refine to the select forms, with IEEE and preserve-sign denormals (unfrozen, an undef x breaks it). New MLIR bench kernel relu6_vec4 (vector<4xf32> maximumf / minimumf): 23 -> 19 instructions, tput 10.9 -> 8.5 (-22.0%); bias_relu_2d_f16 14 -> 13, tput 5.5 -> 5.1 (-7.3%); nothing else changed. agx_run, E398 vs E399: relu6_vec4 over random bit patterns, 30% +-0 / +-inf / NaNs / +-denormals / 6 and its neighbours, and normals x5; bias_relu_2d_f16 over random halves, special halves, and sums cancelling to +-0: 8/8 bit-identical. Test: relu-zero-select.ll (a minnum with -1 keeps the select). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E400 | **Adopted: AIR's portable minimum / maximum recovered also where a constant operand's bits are folded** (AGXLowerAIR: the zero check's or / and of the operands' bits takes a constant in place of bitcast(c), compared through ConstantFoldCastOperand). AIR has no minimum / maximum: air-nan-min-max writes fmin / fmax, select((bits(a) | bits(b)) << 1 == 0, the zeros' or / and, ...) and the NaN select, which agx-lower-air turns back into llvm.minimum / maximum. With a constant (MLIR's relu: maximumf(x, 0); relu6's minimumf(.., 6)) the bitcast of the constant is folded (or(bits(x), 0)), the match failed, and E399 only recovered part of it after instcombine. Recovered, maximum(x, +0) is one fcmpsel ugt (the NaN taken by the unordered compare) and minimum(m, 6) one fcmpsel ult: MLIR bench relu6_vec4 19 -> 14 instructions, 11 -> 7 GPRs, tput 8.5 -> 5.8 (-31.8%; E398 10.9), bias_relu_2d_f16 13 -> 11, tput 5.1 -> 4.4 (-13.7%); nothing else changed (agx-bench's PTX kernels do not go through AIR). agx_run, E399 vs E400, E399's 8 inputs: identical but for NaN payloads (both NaN: E399 gave the canonical NaN, E400 the NaN operand, as the recovery of an unfolded expansion already does; llvm.maximum leaves the payload open). Test: air/recover-minmax-constant.ll (the previous opt recovers neither). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E401 | **Adopted: exact float sums of small integers times dyadic constants, converted back to integers, done in integers** (AGXCodeGenPrepare exactDyadicSums / DyadicSums, -agx-exact-dyadic-sums). Each float value of a tree of uitofp / sitofp of integers (bounds from computeConstantRangeIncludingKnownBits), dyadic constants and fmul / fadd / fsub / fma / fmuladd is tracked as N 2^E, N an integer in a range; while |N| < 2^24 at a common exponent E >= -126 every float result is exact (no rounding, no denormal to flush: the value is N 2^E whatever the rounding mode), so an fptosi / fptoui (saturating ones where the range fits) of the root is the truncation of N 2^E: N shifted, built in i32 (each scaled operand checked below 2^30), where nothing else uses the tree's values; negative N at a negative E keeps the float code. agx-bench blur_rgba8 (a 3 x 3 Gaussian of RGBA8 bytes, weights 1/16, 1/8, 1/4, rounded by + 0.5: (sum w p + 8) >> 4): 40 converts and 36 fmas become integer adds (iadd with a shifted operand), bytes 930 -> 862, cycles 484.8 -> 437.5, tput 63.8 -> 42.9 (-32.8%; 24 -> 33 GPRs, the groups unchanged); nothing else changed. Checks: the three-tap instance over all 2^24 byte triples and the nine-tap blur on 50M random and 512 extreme pixel sets, C fmaf vs the integer form: 0 mismatches (alive2 times out on the float side); agx_run blur_rgba8, E400 vs E401 and an exact model, 7 image shapes (1x1 to 100x1, 64x40) x random / {0, 1, 254, 255} / all-255 pixels: 21/21 identical. Test: exact-dyadic-sums.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E402 | **Adopted: E401's integer sums in 16 bits where they fit** (DyadicSums Ty, -agx-exact-dyadic-i16). E401 built N in i32: blur_rgba8's bytes, 16-bit halves before, each took a 32-bit register (24 -> 33 GPRs). Where every value of the tree scaled to the root's exponent, and the result, are below 2^15 and every converted input is at most 16 bits, N is built in i16 (the result sign- or zero-extended as its range says): blur_rgba8 bytes 862 -> 706, 33 -> 23 GPRs, cycles 437.5 -> 408.6, tput 42.9 -> 34.2 (-20.3%; -46.4% from E400's 63.8); nothing else changed. agx_run blur_rgba8, E401 vs E402 and the exact model, E401's 21 images: identical. Test: exact-dyadic-sums.ll (i16; @wide's 16-bit inputs in i32). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E403 | **Adopted: E386's rounded exit without max(n, 1) where the loop is only entered for n != 0** (mergeUpCounter: ScalarEvolution::isLoopEntryGuardedByCond(n != 0)). E386's t = s ceil(max(n, 1) / s) took an icmpsel for the max before every entry; MLIR's loops are guarded by n != 0 (the trip's select is tested before the preheader), so it goes: one instruction a loop entry. No benchmark kernel takes the rounded form any more (E391's bounds give <u); agx-loop-fuzz.py kernels change in 9 of 200, and 400 seeds pass. Test: merge-up-counter.ll (rowmax: no umax). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E404 | **Measured: probe kernels for new shapes (no change)**. (1) The AIR round trip: 45 LLVM operations (min / max / maxnum, copysign, rounding, fma, sqrt, exp / log / sin / tanh / pow, fdiv / frem, conversions, ctlz / cttz / ctpop / bitreverse / bswap, min / max / abs / saturating ints, mulhi, fshl, division, umul.with.overflow), scalar and <4 x>, through llc -mtriple=air -air-portable and opt -O3 vs straight to AGX: the same instruction counts after E400 (frem: an exec-mask move). (2) rowcopy_vec4's memrefs as noalias (by hand): its 16 load / store pairs an iteration no longer wait in turn, one group 6777.8 -> 2143.9 cycles, but 12 -> 72 GPRs, 84 -> 22 groups, tput 128.0 -> 299.5: alias versioning would not pay. (3) A vector<4xf32> running arith.maximumf: 5 instructions an element (the select, the NaN select of the load, the and / flush / select of zeros; the result is stored, so E389 does not apply). (4) Dynamic-shape memrefs (memref<?x?xf32>, descriptor calling convention; bench.py's bare pointers cannot pass them): an add of two is 49 instructions, 64-bit index arithmetic from the descriptors' i64 sizes and strides and a 64-bit global id (no upper_bound): left for a narrowing of descriptor indices. (5) i8 dot products keep byte loads (E236's byte exclusion: dot_i8 29 -> 33 GPRs) and two instructions a sign extension (as Mesa). (6) MLIR index division: floordivsi / remsi / ceildivsi / divui by one divisor share one sdiv and one udiv. (7) Welford's mean divides by a float counter 1..n: the full division stays (a counter-range reciprocal would need exhaustive checks near the underflow threshold). | measured | -- |
| L15-E405 | **Adopted: bounded 64-bit arithmetic of zero-extended 32-bit values computed in 32 bits** (AGXCodeGenPrepare narrowBoundedI64, -agx-narrow-bounded-i64; AGXNarrowInductions' upperBound also bounds thread_index_in_simdgroup and simdgroup_index_in_threadgroup by 31). MLIR's index arithmetic of positions is 64-bit. A tree of zext(i32) leaves (bounded by scalar evolution: the positions, E391's upper bounds among them), constants and shl / lshr by constants, add, or, and, mul, every node below 2^32 by the operations' own bounds (and: the smaller operand's; or: the sum's), is the zero extension of the same tree in i32 (nuw), at roots whose users are outside such trees, but not at a root an address uses (a GEP, an inttoptr, or an add / or / sub: an integer address): there zext(x) << s is the access's scaled index, and zext(x << s) loses it (a first version: h2 +6%, relu6_vec4 +3%, silu_f16x4 +1.2%; load-lanes-live.ll's PTX address b + (zext(tid) << 3) became a 64-bit add and copies). MLIR bench saxpy_vec4 tput 48.7 -> 48.2 (-1.0%); nothing else changed (agx-bench unchanged). agx_run, E403 vs E405: saxpy_vec4 (n 4 to 16384, 4 runs) and gemm_simd_matrix (k 16, 64, 128): 7/7 bit-identical. Test: narrow-bounded-i64.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E406 | **Adopted (gate): agx-exact-fuzz.py, a differential fuzz of float arithmetic on converted integers converted back** (llvm/utils/agx-stress; agx-stress.py runs it as fuzz-exact, so nine fuzzers). Kernels of one to four trees: uitofp / sitofp of input bytes and halfwords, constants (dyadic, which E401 may do in integers, and 0.3, 0.1, 1.7, 0.299, 2.5e-3, 1e5, which it must not), fmul / fadd / fsub / fma / fmuladd within four products, often + 0.5, converted by fptosi / fptoui (i16, i32) or their saturating forms. The model is binary32 with each operation rounded once to nearest even (fma from the exact value; constants within 2^-20..2^20, so nothing nears a denormal), 1024 threads of 40% boundary words, out-of-range non-saturating conversions (poison) and fmuladd trees (fused or not) excepted. E401 changes 79 of 200 kernels; with its truncation off by one (N + 1 shifted) 42 of 200 seeds fail; the compiler passes 500. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E407 | **Adopted: E401's exact trees whose value stays a float, in integers and converted once** (exactDyadicSums, -agx-exact-dyadic-float-roots). A root of an exact tree (a user outside exact trees: a box filter's sum of converted bytes, then multiplied by 1/9) with two converted leaves or more, nothing else using the tree's values: N in integers (i16 where it fits), one uitofp, times 2^E (exact: |N| < 2^24, E >= -126); i16 now wherever the values' ranges fit, whatever the leaves' integer types (byte-loads.ll's bytes01, two masked bytes of a word added as floats: 3 -> 2 instructions; with i32 for an i32 leaf it was 5, moves out of the bytes' halves). Only for non-negative trees (no fsub, every node and constant at least 0): a float tree with signs can give -0 where the conversion gives +0 (with the check removed, agx-exact-fuzz.py's stored floats fail 3 of 300 seeds, -0 against +0). agx-exact-fuzz.py now also stores a tree's float bits, or the float times 1/3, compared bit for bit (zeros' signs by IEEE's rules): E407 changes 21 of 300 kernels; the pre-E407 compiler and E407 pass 300. New MLIR bench kernel box3_u8f (a 3-tap box filter of bytes to floats): 18 -> 16 instructions, tput 7.9 -> 6.2 (-21.5%); nothing else changed. agx_run box3_u8f, E405 vs E407, random / {0, 1, 254, 255} / all-0 / all-255 bytes: 4/4 bit-identical. Test: exact-dyadic-sums.ll (@box; @floatsub keeps its fsub). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E408 | **Adopted: E395's fit tests folded into versionGuards' own test for its guard-free copies** (versionTapBases with the copy's ballot, -agx-tap-bases-in-guards). conv3x3's tap loop is versionGuards' copy for SIMD-groups inside the image; E395 versioned it again, and the third copy kept the six stepped pointers, which counted in the kernel's registers (63 GPRs, against 51 for E395's hand edit). The SIMD-groups that fail the copy's test already run the loop it copies, which handles every case: the fit tests now join that test (the ballot's argument, and the fits) and the copy is rewritten in place. agx-bench conv3x3: 63 -> 51 GPRs, 24 -> 32 groups, cycles 15224.5 -> 15153.4, tput 899.2 -> 887.5 (-1.3%; -27.4% from E394's 1222.9), bytes smaller (two loops, not three); nothing else changed. agx_run conv3x3, E391 vs E408 and NumPy, E395's nine shapes: identical, and so with the fit tests forced false (every group in the original guarded loop). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E409 | **Adopted: (c' - e) / (e + c), e an exponential of a non-positive value, as Markstein's quotient step** (AGXCodeGenPrepare reciprocalsAboveOne's quotients, -agx-quotient-above-one; flushing f32). e = exp / exp2 / exp10 / agx.exp2 of a value computeKnownFPClass cannot make positive (-2|x| k: tanh's), c and c' in [1, 2^20], n = c' - e or e + c': the divisor is in [1, 2^21] and the numerator 0 or at least 2^-24 in magnitude (1 - e is exact near e = 1), so with y = E282's correctly rounded 1 / d, q0 = n y, r = fma(-d, q0, n) (exact) and q = fma(r, y, q0) is the correctly rounded quotient with nothing near a denormal: rcp and five fmas for lowerFDIV32's scaling of both operands, its rcp, fmas and NaN and range selects. agx-bench gelu_f16 (bench.h's tanh, (1 - e) / (1 + e) with ex2.approx): bytes 554 -> 430, 23 -> 18 GPRs, cycles 1724.4 -> 1516.6, tput 203.3 -> 145.5 (-28.4%); nothing else changed. agx_run: (1 - e) / (1 + e) as the division and as the step for every e from +0 to 1.125 (1,066,401,793 bit patterns) and 5,242,880 infinities and NaNs: 0 differing bits; (e + 3) / (e + 2) for every e from 2^-7 to 1 (58,720,257): 0; gelu_f16 E408 vs E409 for every half (65536): identical. Test: quotient-above-one.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E410 | **Adopted: an scf.for by 1 between i32 values cast to index is the loop over i32 between them** (convert-gpu-to-air narrow-loops, narrowI32BoundsLoop). With lb and ub both signed casts of i32 (or both unsigned, or one a constant that fits), not both constants, the iterations of `for j = lb to ub` are those of the i32 loop compared signed (unsigned), and j + 1 <= ub does not wrap: exact without the offsets loop's access bounds, and with no trip count select(ub > lb, umin(ub - lb, S), 0) to compute. The i32 iv is cast to index unsigned where it is not negative (a constant lb >= 0, or an iv that every iteration uses as a memref index, out of bounds otherwise), which keeps its addresses' extensions zero extensions; a first version cast it signed and indvars widened the loop to an i64 count that LSR split into a nested loop (worse). New MLIR bench spmv_csr (CSR rows, row offsets from rowptr16.bin; bench.py's launch lines take --map VA=@FILE): 29 -> 21 instructions, cycles 2247.1 -> 2231.4, tput 51.1 -> 47.6 (-6.8%); every other MLIR bench kernel's binary is unchanged. agx_run: spmv (8192 rows of 0-12 entries, empty rows) and spmv_csr (4096 rows of 0-39) E409 vs E410: identical, and equal to a reference loop; a signed loop from lo to hi summing j*j ^ j over 256 rows with negative, empty, INT32_MIN and INT32_MAX bounds: equal to Python's on both. Test: gpu-to-air.mlir (@csr_row, @i32_bounds). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E411 | **Adopted: a loop whose exit pop is followed at once by another pop runs at the level of the construct around it** (AGXLowerControlFlow shareLoopLevels, -agx-share-loop-levels): the loop entry push (if_fcmp r0l, eq, 0.0, 0.0, 1) and the exit's pop are left out where the exit block holds only that pop and falls through to a block that starts with pops (or holds two pops itself), the preheader holding the push and the function no calls. With the push, a thread at level v > 0 is at v + 1 in the loop and one that leaves it at 1, and the two pops take them to v - 1 and 0; without, they are at v and 1 and the one pop gives the same levels. The active threads in the loop are the same: its while (n = 1) only changes active threads, and each construct inside pushes every inactive thread deeper before it tells levels apart (an else toggles level 1). Code after the loop in its construct, or a loop at the top level, keeps the level. agx-bench: 21 kernels changed, each by these two instructions; tput better in 16 (reduce_shfl g13g -6.6%, spmv_csr -5.4%, spmv_csr_vector -5.2%, ...; geomean of the 21 -1.8%), worse in 5 by 0.1-0.7% (conv3x3, ddot, q4_matvec, q8_matvec, sgemm_reg4x4: cycles and bytes better, the throughput run's timing; q4_matvec differs from E410 only in the push and pop). MLIR bench: 10 changed, tput better in 9 (spmv_csr 47.6 -> 46.0, softmax_rows 190.4 -> 185.3, rowsum_f16 271.2 -> 266.3, ...), saxpy_vec4 48.2 -> 49.0. agx-cf-fuzz and agx-loop-fuzz seeds 1000-1599: 0 failing; with the following-pop condition dropped (any lone exit pop), cf-fuzz fails 13 of 200 seeds. Test: share-loop-levels.ll (@in_if, @then_else, @top). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E412 | **Adopted: an if without an else whose pop is followed at once by another pop is a while at the level around it** (AGXLowerControlFlow shareIfLevels, -agx-share-if-levels): its compare becomes while_* (n = 1), which deactivates the active threads where it fails and leaves inactive ones alone, and one of the two pops is left out (E411's argument: with the if, a thread at v > 0 goes to v + 1 and a failing one to 1, two pops give v - 1 and 0; with the while, v and 1, one pop gives the same; the active threads inside are the same). Not for an if with an else (its flow block holds the else, which tells the failing threads from inactive ones by level 1), nor in functions with calls; inner ifs go first, so a run of ifs ending together shares one level. agx-bench: 5 kernels changed, one pop each: tput spmv_csr_vector -3.9%, spmv_csr -2.3%, argmax -0.4%, attn_decode -0.1%, index_add g13g +0.45% (g14c unchanged); MLIR bench block_reduce one instruction fewer, tput unchanged. agx-cf-fuzz and agx-loop-fuzz seeds 2000-2599: 0 failing; sharing every if without an else whose flow block pops (no following pop required): cf-fuzz fails 56 of 100 seeds. Test: share-if-levels.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E413 | **Adopted: two ifs on the same condition, the first's join only phis, are one if** (AGXCodeGenPrepare mergeSameConditionIfs, -agx-merge-same-condition-ifs). A: br c, T1, M; T1: ...; br M; M: phis; br c, T2, J; T2: ...; br J (same c, same side, single-block bodies, T2 without convergent calls): M's branch takes T2 exactly when A's took T1, so T2's code runs at the end of T1 with T1's values of M's phis, A branches to J, and M's phis become phis of J (J's incoming values from M take A's, from T2 T1's). JumpThreading does this but is off on targets with branch divergence (it runs in the PTX front end's -O2 and in opt for agx and does nothing). The shape is the PTX front end's guarded loads of block reductions (v = c ? sv[i] : -inf; w = c ? si[i] : 0). agx-bench: argmax 128 -> 124 instructions, tput 319.2 -> 316.7 (-0.8%); softmax_online tput 157.4 -> 155.5 (-1.2%); nothing else changed; MLIR bench unchanged. agx_run: argmax and softmax_online, 3 rows of 32000, 2048, 1000, 37 and 1 floats (ties) at 256, 128 and 96 threads: E412 vs E413 identical. agx-cf-fuzz now also makes pairs of ifs on one condition whose bodies load a table entry (unspeculatable; 37 of seeds 0-99 reach the merge): seeds 0-299 0 failing on E412 and E413; with the new phis of J given swapped incoming values, 23 of 100 fail. Test: merge-same-condition-ifs.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E414 | **Adopted: sext(a) * zext(b), b not negative, as sext(a) * sext(b)** (AGXCodeGenPrepare signedWideMuls, -agx-signed-wide-muls): for an i64 mul of a sign-extended i32 and a zero-extended i32 that is zext nneg or known not negative, a sign extension of b next to the mul replaces the zext (the same value), so the selector sees two sign extensions: one imadd with .sx sources (and the addend folded) instead of the unsigned product, an asr of a and an imadd correcting the high word, which it also emits when the zext is in another block (an i64 register; a .td pattern for zext nneg never matched, even in one block). The PTX front end's int dimensions are zext nneg after their guards: sgemm_reg4x4's tile loads ((row + by) * K) 22 -> 18 instructions a trip, tput 2456.4 -> 2262.7 (-7.9%); q4_matvec 295.3 -> 291.7 (-1.2%), q8_matvec 207.6 -> 204.9 (-1.3%); nothing else changed (MLIR bench unchanged). alive2: the i8 -> i16 form (zext nneg, and zext of b & 127) correct (i64 times out); agx_run: sgemm_reg4x4 at 128^3, 64 x 192 x 64, 192 x 64 x 256, 128 x 128 x 16, q4_matvec and q8_matvec at 64 x 4096, 40 x 1024 and 8 x 32: E413 vs E414 identical (sgemm within 5e-5 of a float64 product). Test: signed-wide-muls.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E415 | **Adopted: E389's zero-sign-free min / max through SIMD-group shuffles and threadgroup memory** (AGXCodeGenPrepare ZeroSignFree, ThreadgroupAccesses). A float's bits that only move between lanes (bitcast to i32, shuffles, bitcast back; or a float shuffle) are the moved floats' values; a threadgroup store of the value is free of its zero's sign when every threadgroup load that may read it is: in a function whose threadgroup memory nothing else reads or writes (no calls but intrinsics without memory effects and the barrier, no generic accesses, no atomics), each access is placed within one threadgroup global (G + GEP offsets of known signed index ranges, inttoptr(X + ptrtoint G) with X's known unsigned range, or a scalar evolution X - ptrtoint G bounded by NarrowInductions' upperBound) or else may access any; the ranges use the special registers' (added for the analysis and taken off, rangedCompares') and the dominating branches' conditions (a lane-0 store at (tid >> 5) * 4). softmax_online's running maximum (bench.h shfl_xor reductions, sm[w] = m under l == 0, sm[l] reloaded, exp(x - m)): its five maxnums lose the and / x 1.0 / select fix, 231 -> 201 instructions, tput 155.5 -> 132.9 (-14.5%); nothing else changed (attn_decode's loads step by loops whose trip counts SCEV does not bound, so they may read anything and its ws sums reach a division: unchanged). agx_run: softmax_online E414 vs E415 over 40 launches (n 1..4096, 32..1024 threads; normal values, all +-0, +-0 / denormals / +-1 mixes, non-positive with zeros, inf and NaN): identical. Test: zero-sign-free-threadgroup.ll (@shuffled, @through_threadgroup; @sign_read and @other_unbounded keep the fix, @other_bounded does not). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E416 | **Adopted: a guarded threadgroup load within its global runs unconditionally, a select for the if** (AGXCodeGenPrepare speculateThreadgroupLoads, -agx-speculate-threadgroup-loads; late in the pass, so the folds before it see the phis). A: br c, T, J; T: threadgroup loads and speculatable arithmetic; J: one phi (v = c ? sm[l] : x, block reductions' reloads): when each load's address stays within its global at A's end (E415's ThreadgroupAccesses placement, without T's condition: special-register ranges, dominating branches), T's code runs in A and the phi is a select. Threadgroup globals are allocated for the whole threadgroup, and a load racing a store reads an undefined value the select then drops: an if, its skip and pop for a select. With two phis (argmax's merged value / index reloads, softmax_online's m / s) the register allocator duplicated selects in the next shuffle stage (argmax 788 -> 812 bytes, 11 -> 12 GPRs, tput unchanged), so one phi only. agx-bench: layernorm_f16 159 -> 152 instructions, tput 120.1 -> 114.0 (-5.1%); rmsnorm 99 -> 96, 103.4 -> 101.3 (-2.0%); nothing else changed; MLIR bench unchanged. agx_run: rmsnorm and layernorm_f16, 2 rows of n = 4096, 1000, 64, 2, 1 at 256, 128, 96, 32 and 1024 threads: E415 vs E416 identical (rmsnorm within 2e-7 of a float64 reference). Deep fuzz of E415 (int, fp, half, f64, mem, simd, exact, loop, cf, seeds 3000-3399): 0 failing. Test: speculate-threadgroup-loads.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E417 | **Adopted: E386's up-counter merge for a step invariant in the loop but not constant** (AGXNarrowInductions mergeUpCounter, -agx-merge-up-counters-variable-step). c = 0, s, 2s, ... beside i = i0, i0 + s, ... with s a loop invariant (the two steps the same scalar evolution: LSR's index steps by its own trunc of the stride): c' != n is i' != i0 + n always; c' <u n is i' <u i0 + n where upperBound(i0) + upperBound(n) + upperBound(s) < 2^32 (no round-up form: it needs a constant power of two). MLIR's grid-stride loop (from the grid position by block_dim * grid_dim, narrowed): one iadd a trip fewer, tput 20.1 -> 19.6 (-2.5%; one-group cycles 519.1 -> 523.1); nothing else changed (LLVM bench unchanged). agx_run: grid_stride E416 vs E417 for n = 0, 1, 4097, 65536, 300000, 1000000, 2^20, 2^40, 2^64 - 5 at 256, 1024, 4096, 65536 threads: identical. agx-loop-fuzz now also steps by (a & 63) + 1: seeds 0-1499 0 failing on E417 (0-59 on E416); without the bound (merging with i0 + n possibly wrapping), 22 of 400 fail. Test: merge-up-counter.ll (@grid_stride). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E418 | **Rejected: vector.load / vector.store indices bounding narrow-loops' offsets (and narrowed in loops)**. saxpy_vec4's grid-stride loop of vector<4xf32> keeps a 64-bit index and a 64-bit signed compare (16 instructions a trip): taking vector.load / store like memref.load in loopOffsetBound, and narrowing their indices in loops, gave 10 a trip, 20 -> 12 GPRs, tput 49.0 -> 24.2 (-51%), identical agx_run results over 40 in-bounds launches (narrowing their indices outside loops lost h2's index shift folded into the access: +1 instruction in h2, relu6_vec4, silu_f16x4, so loops only). Not sound: the vector dialect permits out-of-bounds vector.load / store (target specific, unspecified values / memory, not undefined behaviour), and this lowering emits plain GEPs and accesses, so iterations past the memref are defined and the clamped extent would drop their other effects; a narrowed store index could wrap into the buffer. (Declaring out-of-bounds vector accesses unsupported on AGX, inbounds GEPs, would make it sound; a semantics decision, not taken.) | rejected | -- |

Its warp loops' pointers step by loop (unrolled by 8 and 2 over nw = blockDim >> 5), and the threadgroup loads that may read the stored maximum include ws's sums, which reach a division, unless each is placed in its global: scalar evolution's constant maximum trip count is 2^29 - 1 (-8 + 8 (tpt /u 256) wraps unguarded), and applyLoopGuards rewrites tpt to 32 umax tpt from another guard, not the loop's n & -8 != 0, so upperBound with the guarded symbolic maximum (tried) bounds nothing more and no kernel changed. | measured | -- |
| L15-E420 | **Adopted: b + c_j as an earlier b + c_i plus the difference, so that c_j's mov_imm goes** (AGXFoldOperands chainConstantAdds, -agx-chain-constant-adds; machine SSA). Additions of wide constants (mov_imm, no 8-bit immediate) to one base in a block: each after the first takes the nearest earlier sum plus c_j - c_i (mod 2^32, exact) when that is an 8-bit immediate, a negated one (isub), or the register of a constant mov_imm'd earlier in the block or in the entry block (one that stays first), and is rewritten only when every use of c_j's register is (then the mov_imm goes); plans gather over the function (transpose_smem's two bases share 8192 k). Philox's key schedule k0 + r 0x9E3779B9: 113 -> 105 instructions, bytes 680 -> 632, cycles 227.2 -> 219.7, tput 50.9 -> 50.7; MLIR bench stencil5_2d 27 -> 25 instructions, tput 9.3 -> 8.8 (-5.4%), transpose_smem 46 -> 44, tput 17.3 -> 16.9 (-2.3%; one-group cycles 486.8 -> 555.9, the chain is serial); nothing else changed. agx_run: philox (6 seeds, n 4096 and 1000), transpose_smem (1024^2, equal to NumPy's transpose) and stencil5_2d: E417 vs E420 identical. agx-int-fuzz now also makes runs of K + i d additions to one value (19 of seeds 0-199 reach the rewrite): seeds 0-999 0 failing; with the difference's sign reversed, 16 of 200 fail. Test: chain-constant-adds.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E421 | **Adopted: a phi of the same binary operation of the same operands from each predecessor is that operation in the phi's block** (AGXCodeGenPrepare mergePhiOperations, -agx-merge-phi-operations; InstCombine's phi folding, which llc does not run). phi [x op y, A], [x op y, B] (one instruction may come in on several edges) becomes x op y at the join with the flags all had (x and y dominate every predecessor, so the join); an original whose only user was the phi goes; not for divisions and remainders (one with other uses would be computed twice). | L15-E426 | **Adopted: E405's narrowing of bounded 64-bit trees again once the counters are narrowed** (AGXNarrowInductions calls AGX::narrowBoundedI64 after splitting wide counters, -agx-narrow-bounded-after-counters). AGXCodeGenPrepare sees MLIR's 64-bit index j = lane + c as a tree of a 64-bit phi; after loop strength reduction and the counter's narrowing it is zext(c32) + zext(lane): narrowed, a mask j <= row compares 32-bit values (one icmpsel selecting the masked value, not a 64-bit add and two selects). Taking 64-bit phis as leaves in AGXCodeGenPrepare instead (tried: their truncations, for compares that select only) cut the instructions too, but loop strength reduction then dropped the load's own index induction: causal_rowmax one-group cycles 7056.2 -> 8328.6 (tput 573.2 -> 469.8), and with compares that branch as well fir_taps, matmul, rowsum, dot_vec4, rowcopy_vec4 regressed.
