Test: narrow-bounded-after-counters.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E427 | **Adopted: 64-bit compares of values in [-2^31, 2^31) as 32-bit compares** (AGXCodeGenPrepare narrowSignedCompares, -agx-narrow-signed-compares, right after E405's narrowing of bounded trees). An i64 icmp whose operands are constants, sign extensions of at most 32 bits, and / or / xor of such values, or values whose signed range (ConstantRange through add, sub, mul, shl, zext and sext, intersected with known bits and the dominating branches, with the special registers' grid ranges and the kernel's upper bounds as temporary range attributes: addSpecialRegisterRanges gains ForAnalysis) lies in [-2^31, 2^31), is the same predicate on the truncations: every predicate agrees, since sign and order are those of the low words. MLIR's padded stencil probe (zext(x) - 1 against sext(n), x < 1024): 143 -> 130 instructions; MLIR bench stride_loop 25 -> 22 instructions, tput 17.2 -> 16.3; the LLVM bench is unchanged. agx_run: stride_loop E426 vs E427 for 17 n (0 to 2^31 - 1, -1, -1024, -2^31): identical. agx-int-fuzz gains 64-bit compares of masked, offset values (half of them of the thread's position, kernels now carry an upper bound of 1024): without the range check 10 of seeds 0-199 fail; seeds 0-999 0 failing, agx-loop-fuzz 0-299 and agx-mem-fuzz 0-199 0 failing. The new op found a crash older than E409: storeBytesTogether went on to its byte roots after two pieces of one byte (an or of x & 1 with x % 5 | 0x7fff) had left a byte empty; it now gives up there. Tests: narrow-signed-compares.ll, vector-memory.ll @byte_overlap. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E428 | **Adopted: load results pending across control flow** (AGXInsertWaits, -agx-cross-block-waits). A request's lanes are those of its issue (agx_run: the register writes of a load land at the wait on its group, to the lanes active at the issue), so its result may stay pending past an exec mask change: a block whose successors are all later in the layout and reached from no later block (an if's side and join, not a loop's header) passes its pending registers and counts on instead of waiting at its end; a block takes the union of its predecessors' (the counts' maximum), needed at its first touch. A block that ends the function takes them only when it reads or overwrites one before its end (stencil's exit, which touches none, otherwise waited again before its stop after the side's wait: cycles 181.1 -> 199.9, as E61 found). New MLIR bench conv3x3_pad (a zero-padded 3 x 3 convolution of a 1024 x 1024 image, nine bounds-guarded loads whose sums follow the last if): 122 -> 108 instructions, cycles 1327.4 -> 658.5 (the nine loads overlap instead of one latency each), tput 53.7 -> 49.7 (-7.4%); nothing else in either bench changed (E61: their guarded loads are used in their own blocks). agx_run: conv3x3_pad E427 vs E428 over the whole image: identical, and equal to NumPy's fma chain. Sabotage (successors ignore the pending results they take): agx-cf-fuzz 93 of seeds 0-199 and 25 agx-stress kernels fail. agx-cf-fuzz 0-599, agx-loop-fuzz 0-399, mem, simd, int, fp, exact, half and f64 0-199: 0 failing. Test: cross-block-waits.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E429 | **Adopted: 64-bit operations that are only truncated, in 32 bits** (AGXCodeGenPrepare narrowTruncatedOps, -agx-narrow-truncated-ops, after E427). E427's compares truncate their operands in the compares' blocks; a 64-bit add, sub, mul, and, or, xor or shl by less than 32 in another block, all of whose users truncate it to i32, is rebuilt as the 32-bit operation of its operands' low words (exact modulo 2^32; a zero or sign extension of an i32 is that i32), repeated as its operands' new truncations make them such operations: conv3x3_pad's zext(x) - 1 and zext(x) + 1 lose their high words (and the zero high words of x and y their moves): cycles 658.5 -> 652.4, tput 49.7 -> 47.9 (-3.6%), instructions unchanged at 108; nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: conv3x3_pad E428 vs E429 over the image: identical. agx-int-fuzz: 18 of seeds 0-199 change; sabotage (lshr and ashr narrowed too) 55 of 200 fail; seeds 0-999 0 failing, and the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449, loop 0-299) 0 failing. Test: narrow-truncated-ops.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E430 | **Adopted: E429's narrowing past dead trees and truncated extensions** (narrowTruncatedOps first replaces trunc(zext / sext of an i32) by the i32 and deletes trivially dead instructions). E405's narrowing of bounded trees leaves the 64-bit trees it replaced dead until instruction selection, and its zero extension of the 32-bit result truncated by E427's compares: conv3x3_pad's zext(x) + 1 and zext(y) + 1 had those dead ors as users and stayed 64-bit. conv3x3_pad: 108 -> 104 instructions, 24 -> 22 GPRs (64 -> 72 resident groups), cycles 652.4 -> 642.6, tput 47.9 -> 45.2 (-5.6%); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: conv3x3_pad E429 vs E430 over the image: identical, equal to NumPy. agx-int-fuzz 1000-1599, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449, loop 0-299): 0 failing. Test: narrow-truncated-ops.ll @dead_user. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E431 | **Adopted: congruent counters merged** (AGXNarrowInductions mergeCongruentCounters, -agx-merge-congruent-counters, after the loops' narrowing, element indices and merged exits, and again after split wide counters). Two integer phis of a block whose incoming values from each predecessor are one value, or each the phi itself plus the same constant (an increment in the same block), are equal on every path: the later one's uses take the first, and its increment is the first's (moved before it where needed, with the flags both have). MLIR's argmax over a row: the loop's 64-bit load index, narrowed to an element index i.lo, beside the index_cast's i32 copy of the same counter (the argmax's value): one add and one register fewer. New MLIR bench argmax_rows (a thread's first maximum's index over 256 floats): 32 -> 30 instructions, 14 -> 13 GPRs, tput 450.2 -> 437.1 (-2.9%; E429/E430 had moved it 448.0 -> 450.2 by scheduling); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: argmax_rows E430 vs E431 on 5 inputs (normal, zeros, all -inf, ties of small integers, a NaN pattern): identical, and equal to a Python first-maximum. agx-loop-fuzz gains an i32 twin of the counter in the hash, stepped by s or by 2s + 1 (32 of seeds 0-199 change with E431; sabotage merging different steps: 15 of 200 fail, none before the twin); seeds 0-1199 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449) 0 failing. Test: merge-congruent-counters.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E432 | **Adopted: E135 revisited, short bodies with plain loads run with an empty execution mask** (AGXLowerControlFlow isShortALURun, -agx-unskipped-loads: a load that is not a store, atomic or volatile access counts as one instruction of the 4 that -agx-max-unskipped-instrs allows; stores still take the jump). E135 was rejected on B31's hardware numbers (a masked device_load + wait 10-17 ns against 1-4 ns for masked ALU instructions) when the simulator did not model them; it now does (MASKED_DEVICE_LOAD_NS, the taken forward jump's latency and scheduler hold, and the untaken jump's issue), and the jump still loses: where a lane enters the body it is pure cost, and where none does its taken latency exceeds the masked load's. cross-block-waits.ll's two guarded loads, one SIMD-group: every lane out 136.6 -> 71.8 cycles (tput 8.5 -> 7.6), every lane in 161.7 -> 159.7 (10.4 -> 9.1). Counting a load as two instructions (tried) leaves agx-bench unchanged. agx-bench: softmax_online tput 132.9 -> 131.5, argmax and softmax_online cycles -1, argmax / conv3x3 / softmax_online bytes -6 / -54 / -6, nothing worse; MLIR bench: conv3x3_pad 104 -> 94 instructions, cycles 642.6 -> 596.6, tput 45.2 -> 38.1 (-15.7%: its nine guards always have a lane in, as a halo's do), block_reduce tput 22.1 -> 21.4, nothing else changed. agx_run: conv3x3_pad over the image and block_reduce on two inputs, E431 vs E432: identical. agx-cf-fuzz changes 113 of seeds 0-299 (mem and loop none); cf 0-1499, loop 0-399 and the others 0-199: 0 failing. Dropping a jump changes no thread's work by construction (the jumps only skip code no lane runs), so there is no sabotage of the transformation itself; the empty-mask semantics of loads are agx-tv's E135 proof. Test: skip-jumps.ll @guarded_load. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E433 | **Adopted: short bodies with plain device stores run with an empty execution mask too** (isShortALURun, -agx-unskipped-device-stores: a store that is not atomic or volatile and whose memory operands are all device memory). B31 measured a masked device_store at 7-10 ns (the simulator's MASKED_DEVICE_STORE_NS); threadgroup stores stay jumped over (their masked cost is unmeasured: the simulator charges them as active), and allowing them too (tried) made bitonic_smem's one-group cycles 13415.7 -> 13503.1 for its tput 474.4 -> 436.8. agx-bench: saxpy tput 4.9 -> 3.9, cycles 116.5 -> 115.5, q4_matvec tput 291.7 -> 289.4, six kernels' code -6 bytes; q8_matvec tput 204.9 -> 206.2 at its 32 resident groups, where the dropped jump over its lane-0 store was never taken: over 4 to 64 groups in steps of 4 the mean is 257.51 -> 257.00 cycles per group (worse at 6 of 16 counts), the interleaving of groups, not the masked store. MLIR bench: shfl_sum tput 9.1 -> 8.4, warp_reduce 9.3 -> 8.6, block_reduce cycles -1; nothing else changed. agx-cf-fuzz gains stores to the thread's output slots (guarded device stores under its ifs; 52 of seeds 0-299 change with E433, none before): seeds 0-1499 0 failing, and the other fuzzers 0-199 (loop 0-399). Tests: skip-jumps.ll (@memory, @tg_store, NOSTORES); branch-low-bit-xor.ll, control-flow.ll, sign-test.ll and simd-reductions.ll regenerated (the jump gone), cold-regions.ll's @maybe and cross-block-waits.ll's @exit_unused given bodies long enough to keep theirs. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E434 | **Adopted: up to 12 instructions run with an empty execution mask instead of 4** (-agx-max-unskipped-instrs 4 -> 12). The limit 4 came from B31's worst masked ALU cost (2.3 ns, about 3 cycles) against the jump's issue; the jump the bodies avoid costs that issue wherever a lane enters, and a taken jump's ~34 cycles (cold-regions.ll) where none does, against 12 instructions' ~12 cycles at B31's median masked cost (~38 at its worst). Sweep of the limit with E433: 6 and 8 only int8_dp4a_gemv (tput 93.6 -> 92.1); 12 also jacobi2d (cycles 814.7 -> 813.7) and permute4d (cycles 1258.9 -> 1246.9, tput 291.0 -> 285.5), nothing worse; 16, 24 and 32 add attn_decode cycles 26304.4 -> 26285.5 but its tput 2266.1 -> 2268.7: 12 taken. The MLIR bench is unchanged at every limit. agx-cf-fuzz changes 123 of seeds 0-299, agx-loop-fuzz 54 of 0-199: cf 0-1499, loop 0-399 and the other fuzzers 0-199 0 failing. Tests: loop-carried-order.ll and mul64-shl-stride.ll regenerated (the jump gone), cold-regions.ll's @maybe an atomic add (always jumped over), cross-block-waits.ll at a limit of 4. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E435 | **Adopted: E405's bounded trees take subtractions that cannot borrow and umin / umax** (AGX::narrowBoundedI64, -agx-narrow-bounded-sub-minmax). A subtraction a - b whose minuend's least value (scalar evolution's unsigned range) is at least b's bound has a's bound; umin / umax the smaller / larger bound, rebuilt as the i32 intrinsic; umin(a, c) of an a whose bound is at most the constant c is a. MLIR's loop from the lane to n in steps of 32 has the trip umin(n - lane, n) in 64 bits: one 32-bit subtraction, no high words, no umin. New MLIR bench xent_rows (cross-entropy per row of 1000 logits by a SIMD-group: maximumf, the sum of exponentials, lane 0's log(sum) + max - x[label]): 148 -> 146 instructions, cycles 7282.1 -> 7276.9, tput 479.7 -> 475.0 (-1.0%); causal_rowmax tput 485.6 -> 484.1; nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: xent_rows E434 vs E435 on two inputs (normal x 3 and x 30): identical, within 6.6e-7 of NumPy's float64 loss. agx-int-fuzz gains umin / umax (c - zext(x & m), k) in 64 bits with c from 5 to 65536 (borrowing or not), truncated or kept (11 of seeds 0-299 change; sabotage without the borrow check 17 of 300 fail); seeds 0-1499 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449, loop 0-299) 0 failing. Tests: narrow-bounded-i64.ll @lane_trip, @borrow. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E436 | **Rejected: device loads moved up in regions reverted to their original order** (AGXOriginalOrderStrategy taking a ready device load within the next 8 instructions first). gemm_smem_tile's loop is reverted by E239's occupancy rule (the generic order hoists its sixteen threadgroup loads ahead of their fmas), which also undoes the generic order's pairing of its two device loads: load, wait, threadgroup store, load into the same register, wait, store, two latencies. Moved up, the loads issue together (one wait each, groups 0 and 1) at the same 17 GPRs and 96 resident groups: gemm_smem_tile cycles 5248.4 -> 5068.9 but tput 100.6 -> 102.7, matrixMul cycles 5253.1 -> 5072.6 but tput 109.1 -> 110.7, q4_matvec cycles 4319.3 -> 5468.9 and tput 289.4 -> 291.3. Resident groups already hide the second latency; reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E437 | **Adopted: up to 16 load requests outstanding instead of 8** (AGXInsertWaits, -agx-max-pending-loads). The limit of eight had no measurement behind it; B34 measured 16 independent loads in flight behind one wait, and a request beyond what the hardware tracks would stall its issue, not change a result. Sweep: 12 already gives conv3x3 cycles 15150.4 -> 10762.7 (tput 893.2 -> 878.0) and q8_matvec 2662.8 -> 2504.4 (tput 206.2 -> 198.1) but conv3x3_pad tput 38.1 -> 38.5; 16 conv3x3 the same, q8_matvec cycles 2577.2 (tput 201.9), blur_rgba8 cycles 408.6 -> 405.9, conv3x3_pad cycles 596.6 -> 575.6 (tput 38.0), rowsum_f16 90 -> 88 instructions; 32 conv3x3_pad cycles 615.3: 16 taken, nothing worse in either bench. agx-mem-fuzz gains bursts of 9-14 word loads at data-dependent offsets ((a >> s) & 7) * 4, all in flight before their xor (148 of seeds 0-299 change; none did before); sabotage (the limit's wait made a clear of the group, at a limit of 8): agx_run fails on it; seeds 0-1499 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-199 (cf 0-599, loop 0-399) 0 failing. Test: max-pending-loads.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E438 | **Adopted: a kernel's trailing fences go** (AGXLowerControlFlow removeTrailingFences, -agx-remove-trailing-fences). A fence orders the thread's accesses before it against those after it; in a kernel, one that no memory access, barrier or call follows on any path to the stop (a greatest fixpoint over the blocks) orders nothing, nor does the wait of an acquire's sequence (wait 0, then f59d) there, nor an explicit wait (AGXInsertWaits, after, still waits before the stop for load results in flight). Barriers count as accesses: a fence before one orders this thread's stores for the threads past it. Callable functions keep theirs (the caller's accesses follow). MLIR's memref.atomic_rmw is an acq_rel atomicrmw: the release before the atomic stays, its acquire after (wait 0, memory_barrier 3, 1, 9) at the end of a histogram goes. MLIR bench: hist 9 -> 7 instructions, cycles 560.3 -> 419.9, tput 11.1 -> 8.6 (-22.5%); histogram_atomic 21 -> 19, cycles 981.5 -> 848.1, tput 15.0 -> 13.5 (-10%); agx-bench unchanged (its CUDA atomics are relaxed). agx_run: hist and histogram_atomic E437 vs E438: identical, equal to NumPy's bincount. The functional simulator gives fences no effect, so the fuzzers cannot see a wrong removal: the argument is the memory model's (a fence with nothing after it). Tests: trailing-fences.ll (the acquire gone; kept before a store after the join and before a barrier); fences.ll, atomics.ll and simd.ll run with the removal off (their kernels end in the fences they check); known-high-zero-index.ll regenerated. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E439 | **Adopted: E206's down-counters also by a step d > 1** (AGXNarrowInductions mergeDownCounter, -agx-merge-down-counters-by-step). An unrolled loop's LSR counter c = c0, c0 - d, ... (d the unroll factor) from a constant c0 that d divides reaches zero after T = c0 / d iterations: the exit test c' = 0 is the index's i' = i0 + s T (s T folded), with E206's condition for an even s (T below 2^(32 - m)). New MLIR bench colsum (a thread per column of a 512 x 1024 matrix, the constant trip unrolled by 8 before llc): the loop's isub goes (an iadd and a mov_imm before it), cycles 12273.4 -> 12268.1, tput 476.2 -> 458.2 (-3.8%); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: colsum E438 vs E439: identical, and equal to NumPy's sequential sum. agx-loop-fuzz gains constant trips of 8 to 256 in steps of 1 (opt -O2's seeds unroll them; the count of seeds changed counts those too now: 40 of 0-299 change); sabotage (T one more) fails all 40; seeds 0-1499 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449) 0 failing. Test: merge-down-counter.ll @colsum4. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E440 | **Adopted: fptrunc to half of one float operation of halves, in half** (AGXCodeGenPrepare halfFromFloat, -agx-half-from-float). fptrunc(op(fpext a, fpext b)) for op in + - * / is op on the halves: float's 24 bits are at least 2 * 11 + 2, so the second rounding never differs (Figueroa), and no such result is a float denormal (the least, a product, is 2^-48); min / max of every kind, fneg, fabs and selects of so narrowed values commute with the rounding (monotonic, signs and NaNs kept), a half-exact constant is its half. An operation of two operations' results is not narrowed: its float operands are not halves. MLIR's mixed precision truncf(maximumf(addf(extf a, extf b), 0)): one fadd16 and one fcmpsel, not fadd32, fcmpsel and the conversion: bias_relu_2d_f16 11 -> 10 instructions, cycles 119.8 -> 117.7, tput 4.4 -> 4.2 (-4.5%); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: bias_relu_2d_f16 E439 vs E440 over every half bit pattern (with random biases): identical. agx-half-fuzz changes 230 of seeds 0-299 (its widen, float op, narrow op); it gains two float operations (or one under a maximumnum with 0) before the narrowing, its model checked against E439 (0 of 200 failing): sabotage narrowing those too fails 32 of 200; seeds 0-1499 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449) 0 failing. Test: half-from-float.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E441 | **Measured: the off-by-default AGX options again, after E427-E440** (as E263 / E320), and some numeric limits. agx-atomic-optimizer: compact tput 75.8 -> 181.9 (worse); agx-simd-reductions: attn_decode tput 2266.1 -> 2061.4 but layernorm_f16, q4 / q8_matvec, reduce_shfl, spmv_csr_vector, shfl_sum and warp_reduce worse; agx-sched-lane-masks: attn_decode / ddot mixed; agx-enable-sink-remat: within one cycle, gemm_simd_matrix tput 87.6 -> 88.1; agx-unsplit-shuffles and agx-cas-batch-try-first: no change; agx-element-aligned-vectors: h2 tput 4.9 -> 4.2, silu_f16x4 83.3 -> 80.4, q4_matvec 289.4 -> 285.0, q8_matvec 201.9 -> 194.4 but its cycles 2577.2 -> 3481.8, and still unmeasured on hardware (R23); agx-uniform-constants: broadly better (adam tput 19.0 -> 16.6, cross_entropy 709.8 -> 615.7, ddot 2333.6 -> 2261.3) but it needs the launcher's preloaded uniforms (A15: on in the PTX front end, off in llc), and agx_cost does not take one MLIR kernel's. Numeric limits, none better at every kernel: agx-hoist-address-offsets 4 / 16, agx-loop-index-bases 8, agx-postra-cluster-wait 16, agx-postra-tg-load-use 24 / 96, agx-postra-tg-load-long-latency 3 / 8 (bitonic_smem tput 474.4 -> 489.4 at 3), agx-cold-regions-percent 5, agx-headroom-gprs 16 / 32. Nothing changed. | measured | -- |
| L15-E442 | **Adopted: i64 minimum / maximum subgroup reductions as two i32 AIR reductions** (convert-gpu-to-air, before the shuffle lowering). gpu.subgroup_reduce maxui / minui / maxsi / minsi of i64, over the SIMD-group or clusters of 4 or 32: the high words' reduction in the operation's signedness (air.simd_max.s / .u and the like), then the unsigned reduction of the low words of the lanes whose high word is that result (the others give the identity, 0 for a maximum, ~0 for a minimum), recombined: the 64-bit order is the high words' and then, among equal ones, the low words' unsigned. Upstream's lowering was a butterfly of five 64-bit xor shuffles (two i32 each) and 64-bit compare-selects: reduce_f16_i64 52 -> 22 instructions, 10 -> 6 GPRs, cycles 299.0 -> 294.5, tput 22.2 -> 16.6 (-25%); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: reduce_f16_i64 E441 vs E442 on 5 inputs (random, high words of 0-3, all high words ~0, zeros, all ones) and a probe of minui / maxsi over the SIMD-group and minsi / maxui over clusters of 4 on 3 inputs (random; high words of 0-2; high words ~0, 2^31, 2^31 - 1 and 0): identical, equal to NumPy's; sabotage (the identities swapped) differs on all 3. Tests: gpu-to-air.mlir @reduce_i64 (@i64add keeps the shuffle path's check). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E443 | **Adopted: E442 for i64 and / or / xor and sums** (convert-gpu-to-air). Bitwise reductions of i64 are each word's i32 reduction; an i64 sum is the high words' i32 sum (modulo 2^32: the sum's high word, less the low words' carries) plus the sums of the low words' two 16-bit halves (below 2^21 over a SIMD-group's 32 lanes: no carry lost), recombined in 64 bits (three air.simd_sum.s.i32). A probe of add and xor over the SIMD-group and add and or over clusters of 4: 61 -> 42 instructions, no shuffles; agx_run E442 vs E443 on 4 inputs (random, all ones, low words all ones, 16-bit halves): identical, equal to NumPy's; sabotage (the low words summed whole) differs on the three whose low words carry. No bench kernel has such a reduction: nothing in either bench changed. Tests: gpu-to-air.mlir @reduce_i64 (add, xor); @i64mul keeps the shuffle path's check. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E444 | **Adopted: a loop's running maximum / minimum with its NaN and zero signs deferred to the exit** (AGXCodeGenPrepare deferredMinMaxReductions, -agx-deferred-minmax-reductions). A header phi m whose latch value is a chain of llvm.maximum (minimum) of m and loop values (an unrolled loop's several), each link used only by the next, the last only by the phi and outside the loop: in the loop m' = v > m ? v : m (a NaN v leaves m), n' = v uno v ? v : n (the last NaN seen, the initial value at first) and z' = smax(z, bits(v)) (smin for minimum), one compare-select each, three instructions for the lowering's five (compare-select, the and of the bits, its flush, the equal select and the NaN select); on each edge out of the loop (a block of its own: SplitEdge) r = n uno n ? n : (m == 0 && z >= 0 (z < 0) ? +0 (-0) : m). That is maximum's value: a NaN if one was seen, else the largest, and for a zero result every value was at most a zero or denormal (which the compares flush), so a clear sign among their bits is a +0 or positive denormal, of which maximum gives +0 or the denormal either way. An i1 flag or'd per value (tried first) took four instructions: the DAG made the select an or of 0 / 1 values. MLIR's maximumf row maxima (torch-mlir's softmax): xent_rows cycles 7276.9 -> 7148.8, tput 475.0 -> 453.2 (-4.6%; 146 -> 149 instructions, the exit's); new MLIR bench rowmax_vec4 (a thread's maximumf over a row of 256 floats in vector<4xf32> steps, unrolled by 4 before llc): 122 -> 110 instructions, cycles 3730.1 -> 3075.0, tput 718.4 -> 550.7 (-23%) though 29 -> 33 GPRs (56 -> 48 resident groups); agx-bench unchanged (its CUDA maxima are maximumnum). agx_run: rowmax_vec4 and xent_rows E440 vs E444 on 6 inputs each (normal; +0 / -0 rows; all -0; NaNs; negative rows with a +0 and some -0; all -inf): identical bits, NaN payloads included, and rowmax_vec4 equal to an exact IEEE maximum. agx-loop-fuzz's running maximum takes maximum and minimum too, bare in the loop as MLIR's, the default NaN only at the store (its model checked against E440: 0 of 300 failing; 41 of seeds 0-299 change with E444); sabotage (the zero sign ignored) fails 6 of 300; seeds 0-1499 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449) 0 failing. Test: deferred-minmax.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E445 | **Adopted (guard): E444 after the zero-sign-free min / max, and not for nsz links** (deferredMinMaxReductions after zeroSignFreeMinMax; a link with nsz ends the chain). An nsz maximum lowers to its compare-select and NaN select, as few instructions as the deferral; an nsz maximumnum to one fcmpsel. Tried with it: E444 for maximumnum / minimumnum from a number (m' = v >= m ? v : m, so a +0 meeting -0 is taken; z' = smax(z, bits(m')), never a NaN's; no NaN carried): the only chain it met, attn_decode's cross-SIMD-group maximum over threadgroup memory, is nsz already (one fcmpsel gtn a value), which it made two (bytes 2332 -> 2424, cycles unchanged); its hot loop's running maximum has other uses in the loop (the online softmax's rescale), and agx-loop-fuzz's maximumnum takes the PTX front end's default NaN each step: no target, not kept. Nothing in either bench changed against E444; agx-loop-fuzz 1500-1999 0 failing. Test: deferred-minmax.ll @nsz. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E446 | **Adopted: E444 for a running maximumnum / minimumnum from a number, its zero-sign-free uses in the loop taking the deferred value** (deferredMinMaxReductions, -agx-deferred-minmaxnum-reductions). From a constant that is not a NaN the running value never is one (maximumnum gives a NaN only of two), so no NaN is carried: m' = v >= m ? v : m (a NaN v leaves m; with >= a +0 meeting -0 is taken), z' = smax(z, bits(m')) (never a NaN's bits), two instructions for the lowering's four (the and of the bits, the compare-select, the flush and the equal select), and after the loop m == 0 && z >= 0 ? +0 : m. Uses of the phi or a link in the loop besides the chain are allowed where ZeroSignFree proves no zero's sign reaches anything (an online softmax's rescale e^(m - m'), the exponent of the new score less m'): they take the deferred value before (after) the link, the same but for a zero's sign; any other use keeps the chain. attn_decode's online softmax, whose maximum ZeroSignFree alone could not free (the merged maximum's threadgroup slot may reach the final division): cycles 26304.4 -> 26005.8, tput 2266.1 -> 2153.9 (-4.9%), bytes 2332 -> 2364 (the exits' results); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: attn_decode E445 vs E446 on 5 inputs (random twice, every score 0, every score -0, scores x 8), 8 heads: identical. agx-loop-fuzz's running maximum is also used in the loop, compared with the value (zero-sign free) or its bits hashed (not), its model checked against E445 (0 of 300 failing; 13 of 0-299 change with E446); sabotage taking every use without ZeroSignFree fails 4 of 400, giving every use the phi's deferred value 5 of 400; seeds 0-1999 0 failing (and with minimumnum running minima from +3e38 added to the fuzzer, its model checked against E445: 19 of 0-299 change, 0-1499 0 failing), cf 0-299 and fp, mem, simd, int 0-149 0 failing. Test: deferred-minmax.ll @online. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E447 | **Adopted: compares of an induction value rebased onto another header phi of its step** (AGXNarrowInductions rebaseCompares, -agx-rebase-compares, at the end of the pass on the final 32-bit compares, with the function's own fresh analyses; then mergeUpCounter). An unsigned (or equality) compare of A = {a0,+,s} (a 32-bit value or its zero extension) with a loop-invariant B is the same compare of a 32-bit header phi I = {i0,+,s} with B + D, D = i0 - a0 invariant (expanded before the loop), where neither A + D nor B + D passes 2^32 (upperBound and scalar evolution's ranges of A, B and D): I = A + D at every iteration, and adding D keeps an unsigned order that does not wrap. MLIR's causal mask j <= row over j = lane + c (64-bit until E426 narrows it, too late for LSR) beside the load's index base + lane + c: the mask compares the index with row + base, j's add goes, and the counter c, left to the exit test, merges into the index (E386; the dead values the rebase left are deleted first, as the one-use test would count them). causal_rowmax 74 -> 71 instructions (each loop two adds fewer), 26 -> 24 GPRs, tput 484.1 -> 440.1 (-9.1%), one-group cycles 6990.4 -> 7023.4 (+0.5%: the first loop's wait follows its load at once, the adds that sat between gone); nothing else in either bench changed. agx_run: causal_rowmax E446 vs E447 on two inputs: identical, within 2.2e-7 of NumPy's float64 sums. agx-loop-fuzz gains attention-row loops (the counter from the lane, the index the row's base plus it, unmasked, the mask counter <= (b & 2047)); 39 of seeds 0-299 change (counter phis as A allowed, the rebased phi another); sabotage without the bounds (which also rebases exit tests that wrap) fails 170 of 400; seeds 0-1499 0 failing, the other fuzzers 0-149 (cf 0-449) 0 failing. Test: rebase-compares.ll (MLIR's causal_rowmax). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E448 | **Rejected: threadgroup stores in short bodies of an if on lane == c** (AGXLowerControlFlow: an AGX_IF_ICMP of sr52, thread_index_in_simdgroup, equal to c < 32 lets the body run with an empty mask also with plain threadgroup stores, whose masked cost E433 found unmeasured: no full SIMD-group skips such a body). block_reduce tput 21.4 -> 21.3, block_scan_simd cycles 404.1 -> 403.1 and 6 bytes, but layernorm_rows tput 46.1 -> 46.6 (its lane-0 stores of the two partial sums, the jump never taken: E433's interleaving, within noise either way). Too little either way; reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E449 | **Adopted: selects and compares of extended halves done on the halves** (AGXCodeGenPrepare halfSelects, -agx-half-selects, after the math and division expansions). select(c, ext a, ext b) is ext(select(c, a, b)) and fcmp(ext a, ext b) is fcmp(a, b) for halves a, b, or on one side a float constant exact in half (the extension is exact and keeps NaNs and the order). The select's extension then folds into the float operations that read it as a 16-bit source (fadd32 / fmul32 / fmadd32 M16 forms) instead of being a conversion before the select. Selects only where every user reads a 16-bit source: float arithmetic, fmas, half truncations, compares and selects turned to half here, and negations of those. -ext(h) becomes ext(-h), since instruction selection matches an extended operand only with the fpext on top. Case: the exp expansion's argument clamps to [-89, 104] in silu's e^-x of an extended half (x + b in f16). silu_f16x4 tput 83.3 -> 81.4, cycles 495.9 -> 456.6, 192 instructions either way: the conversions the division's bit operations need stay, but the clamps no longer wait for them. GPRs 27 -> 28, 55 groups either way. No other MLIR bench kernel and no agx-bench kernel changes. Verified: silu_f16x4's agx_run outputs are bit-identical for all 65536 halves with a zero bias, and for two random biases. agx-half-fuzz gains an ext32sel operation: a compare-select of extended halves or float constants (exact in half and not: 0.1, 1e5, 70000), perhaps clamped again and negated, then a float add or multiply. It differs on 79 of 300 seeds and all pass. Sabotage (inexact constants accepted) fails 9 of 300. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E450 | **Adopted: exp(x - m) below a SIMD-group's integer-ordered maximum of loop maxima** (AGXCodeGenPrepare expBelowLoopMax, now also run at the start of the pass, recording !agx.exp.below.max on the exp for expandMath). Two things kept MLIR's xent_rows out of E270/E387's match: (1) its gpu.subgroup_reduce maximumf (E275/E276) is a signed simd max of the order-preserving encoding (b < 0 ? 0x7fffffff : 0) ^ b, with a NaN as 0x7fc00000, decoded the same way, whose result is a NaN or the largest lane's m, at least this lane's own (orderedSIMDMaxOf requires the NaN select on an uno of m itself; without it a negative NaN would sort below every number); (2) the loop maximum is llvm.maximum of a frozen load, the same value as the second loop's load. The deferred maximum reductions (E444) also rewrite that loop's maximum before expandMath, hence the early mark. xent_rows tput 453.2 -> 405.7, cycles 7148.8 -> 7001.5, 149 -> 144 instructions, GPRs 24 -> 21: the exp's upper clamp and +inf select leave the loop. No other MLIR bench or agx-bench kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: xent_rows outputs bit-identical over 6 seeds of 64 rows of adversarial data (normal and wide values, random bits, NaNs of both signs, infinities, signed zeros, denormals, largest floats, exp's clamp bounds, single specials and all-special rows). Unchanged code for a variant reducing with the signed minimum. Sabotage (any reduction accepted) gives different outputs on every seed of that variant. exp-below-max.ll gains @xent_rows. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E451 | **Adopted: constants read as plain operands reused from any dominating block** (AGXInstrInfo::isAsCheapAsAMove, -agx-cse-constants). MachineCSE reuses an instruction that is as cheap as a move only from its own block or an immediate predecessor, its heuristic against longer live ranges. So the same constant took a mov_imm per if region or loop preheader: conv3x3_pad's bound 1024, compared in nine guarded loads (9 mov_imm after isel, 5 after MachineCSE), and the exp coefficients of softmax_rows' two loops. A MOV_IMM16 / 32 whose uses all read it as an operand is now not cheap, so it is reused from any dominating block. Those read by a phi, a copy, a REG_SEQUENCE / INSERT_SUBREG or a tied operand keep the heuristic: zero accumulators and a masked load's default would take a copy of the one zero instead. Without that exclusion (the first try) nbody tput went 3356.2 -> 3421.1 (34 -> 35 GPRs: copies of one zero), attn_decode 2153.9 -> 2160.5, and argmax_rows, causal_rowmax, rowmax_vec4 and gemm_simd_matrix also got worse. With it: conv3x3_pad tput 38.0 -> 37.1 (93 -> 90 instructions), softmax_rows 185.3 -> 183.9 (121 -> 111), xent_rows 405.7 -> 402.0 (144 -> 141, GPRs 21 -> 23, 55 groups either way), permute4d 285.5 -> 281.7 (bytes 12788 -> 12600). Cycles: ddot 10664.6 -> 10630.6, scan_blelloch 5105.5 -> 5081.3, softmax_online 2372.0 -> 2370.8. Worse: rowsum_f16 tput 266.3 -> 268.6 (88 -> 87 instructions; its loops' instructions are the same, with a different register assignment, and agx_cost --annotate spreads the difference thinly over their contention stalls), ddot tput 2333.6 -> 2334.8, scan_blelloch GPRs 16 -> 17 (tput the same). Adopted for the net gain. A variant also requiring every use in the constant's own block kept conv3x3_pad, xent_rows and permute4d but lost softmax_rows and made softmax_online tput 131.5 -> 132.9. Only operands change, not values. cse-constants.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E452 | **Adopted: minimum / maximum's compare-select and NaN select as one fcmpsel** (AGXISelLowering lowerFMinimumMaximum, -agx-nanprop-select; AGXISD::FMAX_NANPROP / FMIN_NANPROP). fcmpsel's "n" conditions make a NaN operand lose: ltn(a, b) is a < b, or a number a against a NaN b (simulator fsem fcond 3). So ltn(b, a) ? a : b takes a where it is larger or a NaN, b otherwise, b where both are NaNs. That is exactly select(b uno b, b, select(a ugt b, a, b)), the two selects maximum lowered to before, and likewise gtn for minimum. fcmpsel has no invert bit (table.rs: fcond(61, None)), and the swapped selected operands give the inversion. maximum nsz is 2 -> 1 instructions, full maximum 5 -> 4 (its zero fix stays). A non-NaN constant operand keeps the one ugt select. Only rowmax_vec4 changes on its own: 110 -> 107 instructions (the vector reduction's three maximums), cycles 3075.0 -> 3068.6, tput 550.7 -> 550.9. Nothing else, MLIR or agx-bench, uses llvm.maximum outside constants. Verified: on agx_run, 16 variants (f32 / f16, maximum / minimum, nsz or not, both operand orders) over 65536 operand pairs (every pair of 24 f32 and 21 f16 specials, 20000 near-equal pairs, random words) are bit-identical to the previous lowering, NaN payloads included. agx-fp-fuzz gains maximum / minimum (40% nsz): it differs on 109 of 300 seeds and passes 400. Sabotage (f32 maximum as maxnum) gives 280 differences on the pair test and fails 37 of 200 fp-fuzz seeds. minmaxnum.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E453 | **Adopted: the deferred maximum's NaN in its running value** (AGXCodeGenPrepare deferredMinMaxReductions, -agx-deferred-minmax-nan-in-m). E444's loop kept m' = v > m ? v : m and a NaN carrier n' = v uno v ? v : n beside it, two selects. Now m' = maximum nsz(m, v), E452's one fcmpsel ltn(v, m) ? m : v. It keeps a NaN m, takes a NaN v (the last NaN seen, the payload maximum's chain gives, as the carrier did), and takes a v equal to m. After the loop: r = m == 0 && z >= 0 ? +0 : m, with no NaN select (minimum mirrored). Per element 3 -> 2 instructions (fcmpsel ltn, icmpsel sgt). rowmax_vec4 tput 550.9 -> 287.5 (cycles 3068.6 -> 2631.8, 107 -> 83 instructions, GPRs 33 -> 29, 48 -> 56 groups), xent_rows 402.0 -> 391.1. No agx-bench kernel changes (they use maxnum / maximumnum). The one change in values: a v equal to m is taken, where E444 kept the first. That shows only where every value is at most a zero and two or more are zeros or denormals, all of negative sign (positive for minimum). There the result is the last such value instead of the first. Both are the class of result E444 already took from the denormal mode ("+0 or the denormal either way"). While checking this: E444 / E446 already differ from the undeferred lowering there. That lowering's tie gives the canonicalized and of the bits (-0 for two negative denormals) and keeps a lone positive denormal; the deferred forms give a denormal and +0 (both allowed by the denormal mode, as E444 noted; not changed here). Verified on agx_run against the E452 compiler: 40 seeds of 1024 rows of 8 values (specials-only, zeros / denormals-only, mixed random words) for a running maximum and minimum. 3 of 81920 outputs differ, all zero-ish results of the same sign from such rows. Rows with four distinct NaN payloads are identical. rowmax_vec4 on 8192 rows of 256 values (normals, sprinkled specials, special-only and zero-ish rows) is identical. agx-loop-fuzz differs on 42 of 600 seeds. deferred-minmax.ll (CARRY: the E444 form). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E454 | **Adopted: a vector's deferred maxima reduced before their result** (AGXCodeGenPrepare: deferredMinMaxReductions builds the result per element for an extractelement of a vector chain; combineDeferredMinMax; -agx-deferred-minmax-lane-results). E453's result r = m == 0 && z >= 0 ? +0 : m (tagged !agx.deferred.minmax, so its z is known to belong to its m) obeys maximum(r1, r2) = the result of maximum nsz(m1, m2) and smax(z1, z2). Where the larger m is a number, r is that m. For two zero or denormal m's it is +0 if either has a clear sign among its values' bits, as maximum's +0 of +0 and a zero. A NaN is m2's if both are NaN, as maximum's b. The one other case: two all-negative zero / denormal m's, where maximum's canonicalized and gives -0 and the combined result gives m2, both denormal results (E453's class). Minimum mirrored. A vector maximum's lanes reduced after its loop (MLIR vector.reduction <maximumf>: rowmax_vec4) take three nsz maximums, three smax and one result, instead of four results and three maximums of four instructions. rowmax_vec4 tput 287.5 -> 282.6 (83 -> 71 instructions, cycles 2631.8 -> 2611.4). No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run against the E453 compiler: rowmax_vec4 and its minimum variant on 12288 rows (normals, sprinkled specials, special-only, zero-ish-heavy, and rows of -1 (+1) with 2 to 4 zeros / denormals in different lanes) are identical, but for 3 rows each of 10240 targeted ones, which are same-sign zero results. Sabotage (smin for maximum's z) gives 35 differences, 18 of them wrong signs, on the targeted rows. deferred-minmax.ll @vec4. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E455 | **Adopted: exp(x - m) below a masked loop maximum** (AGXCodeGenPrepare expBelowLoopMax, -agx-exp-masked-max). A causal row's maximum is maxnum(m, select(j > row, -inf, x)), and the second loop's exp(x - m) is used only through select(j > row, 0, e). The value taken where the condition is false is the load the maximum took, so x - m <= 0 (or a NaN) there. Elsewhere the exp, now without its upper clamp and +inf select, gives a value the select drops: the expansion's float-to-integer conversion is frozen, so no poison. The match: the maximum's other operand a select of -inf and the (frozen) load. Every use of the exp a select whose condition is a compare with the same predicate of the same functions of the iteration (Same's SCEV correspondence of the two loops), taking the exp on the side where the maximum took the load. causal_rowmax tput 440.1 -> 398.9 (cycles 7023.4 -> 6876.1, 71 -> 66 instructions, GPRs 24 -> 21). No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: causal_rowmax over all 1024 rows, three data sets (specials in the masked part; 1000.0 there, whose exp overflows, with 1% specials in the rest; 10% specials everywhere), is bit-identical to E454. A variant using the exp where the condition is true (select(c, e, 0)) is unchanged. Sabotage (no check of the exp's uses) gives 1024 differences on that variant. exp-below-max.ll @causal_rowmax. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E456 | **Rejected: threadgroup loads modelled as a shorter use of the memory unit** (AGXSchedule.td WriteLocalLoad ReleaseAtCycles 20 -> 4, latency 20 kept). gemm_smem_tile's inner loop issues each B-column threadgroup_load just before the fmadd32 that reads it, and agx_cost charges such a load 47 issue cycles against 28 for one after a load. Hand-scheduled (llvm-mc), the four loads of each step first, then the four fmadds: 5248.4 -> 5176.8 cycles, 2 groups 5279.1 -> 5211.5. Register renaming alone changes nothing (5252.4). But with as many groups as reside, the hand-clustered loop (four loads into four reused registers, 20 GPRs either way) is slower: 24 groups 5987.0 -> 6013.9, 48 groups 6804.8 -> 7188.1. The interleaved order is the better one for throughput, as E239 found for the fully hoisted loads. With the shorter resource use the scheduler still interleaves gemm_smem_tile (unchanged), and elsewhere: sgemm_reg4x4 tput 2271.8 -> 2265.6 but cycles 21276.8 -> 21461.6, layernorm_rows tput 46.1 -> 47.0. Reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E457 | **Adopted: the exp expansion's ldexp as one imadd** (AGXInstructions.td: add(shl(n, 23), bits) with n a float's (frozen) conversion as imadd n, 2^23, bits, 2^23 from a mov_imm). E318 made x + (a << s) one imadd for s of 5 to 7, 2^s an 8-bit immediate. For the integer ldexp's n << 23 (E-series exp: bits(e^r) + (n << 23)) the power needs a register. But the underflow test S < 2^23 already holds that constant, and E451's CSE shares it, so bfi and iadd become one imadd on the same unit (exact: the product's low 32 bits are n << 23). Hand test on cross_entropy: tput 709.8 -> 701.6. A first version for every shift of 8 to 30 also took addresses: sgemm_reg4x4 tput 2271.8 -> 2296.9, its loop-invariant shift no longer hoisted out of the loop. gemm_smem_tile 100.6 -> 103.5 (one more GPR, 96 -> 88 groups), xent_rows and transpose_smem worse. Requiring a shifted value computed in the block did not keep sgemm's out. Hence only n << 23 of a conversion. Results: cross_entropy tput 709.8 -> 701.6, causal_rowmax 398.9 -> 394.2, silu_f16x4 81.4 -> 80.4 (192 -> 188), softmax_rows 183.9 -> 181.5, softmax_row cycles 239.5 -> 238.5. Worse: xent_rows tput 391.1 -> 394.4 (cycles 6961.9 -> 6929.9 and 138 -> 137 instructions better; contention). Adopted for the net gain. Verified: xent_rows' adversarial agx_run set (E450's) is bit-identical. Sabotage (imsub) gives 12 differences there. fp.ll regenerated; imadd-ldexp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E458 | **Measured (open question, no change): the rounding of an fp32 operation with a 16-bit destination.** The backend folds fptrunc(fadd / fmul / fma of f32) into FADD32 / FMUL32 / FMADD32 with a 16-bit destination. AGXInstructions.td cites applegpu's saturate_and_set_thread_result: the result rounded to f32 and that to f16, the double rounding the IR's fptrunc of the operation means. The simulator (fsem.rs float_arith, out16) instead rounds the exact result straight to f16. Its transcendental units do round the fp32 result to fp16 ("recorded tables"). Discriminating case on agx_run: fptrunc(fmul(1 + 2^-12, 1 + 2^-12)), whose exact product 1 + 2^-11 + 2^-24 rounds to the f32 tie 1 + 2^-11 and then to even 1.0 (0x3c00). The compiled fmul32 r2l, r2, r3 gives 0x3c01 in the simulator. The hardware recording coverage/expected/alu/fmul32_r14l_r6_r7 has no lane where the two roundings differ, so it does not decide. Either the fold (gelu_f16's fmul32 r9l, the fused conversions) or the simulator is off by one ulp of half on such inputs. A recording of that form with tie-producing operands would settle it. Not changed here: the bit-identity checks of this work compare compilers that both fold. agx-half-fuzz's ext32 / ext32b operations read extended halves, which the fold excludes (AGXOneUseF32Op), so they never meet it. E449's ext32sel did meet it, with fmul(-select, 0.1) then fptrunc: a deep fuzz (seeds 5000-5400) found 2 outputs one half-ulp apart in the half denormals (0x89 against the model's 0x88), also with -agx-half-selects=0. That operation's last operand is now an extended half, which keeps the fold out; 600 + 600 seeds pass. | measured | -- |
| L15-E459 | **Adopted: x ^ ((x >> 31) & c) as (x < 0) ? x ^ c : x** (AGXInstructions.td pattern, c not an 8-bit immediate). MLIR's maximumf / minimumf subgroup reductions (E275 / E276) encode a float's bits as an order-preserving key and decode the reduced key the same way: an asr, an and with 0x7fffffff and the xor, each time. A bitop xor with c and an icmpsel on the sign is two instructions, on the same units. An 8-bit c keeps its existing one-icmpsel and plus the xor, the same count. xent_rows tput 394.4 -> 382.6 (137 -> 135 instructions; cycles 6929.9 -> 6931.8). No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: seven masks (0x7fffffff, 0x80000000, 0x12345678, 256, 0xff, 0x7f800000, 0xffff) over 65536 random and edge words equal the reference, before and after. Sabotage (sgt) gives 393216 differences. xor-sign-mask.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E460 | **Measured, not kept: a deferred maximum without its sign-of-zero flag where its uses after the loop ignore a zero's sign** (deferredMinMaxReductions: no z and no fix when ZeroSignFree accepts every use outside the loop). No kernel changes: zeroSignFreeMinMax (E389) runs earlier with the same analysis and marks such a maximum nsz, and an nsz link ends E444's chain. attn_decode's running maximumnum is stored to threadgroup memory and merged in a way the analysis rejects, so it keeps z. Reverted. | measured | -- |
| L15-E461 | **Adopted: exp(x - m) below a loop maximum of extended loads** (expBelowLoopMax, -agx-exp-extended-loads). A row of halves read as floats: m the maximum of fpext(freeze(load half)) and x = fpext(load half) of the same addresses. The extension is the same exact, monotone map on both sides, so x <= m (or a NaN) as for float loads. Both sides must extend, or neither. New MLIR bench kernel softmax_f16 (row softmax of f16 logits in f32 by a SIMD-group, three passes, the f16 normalized write; README row) shows it. Its second loop's exp loses the upper clamp and +inf select. The third's keeps them: it stores to y, which may alias x. softmax_f16 tput 771.7 -> 721.5 (cycles 12191.4 -> 12048.5, 113 -> 111 instructions, GPRs 26 -> 23). No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: 4 seeds of 64 rows (normal halves, sprinkled specials, special-only rows, 30% specials: NaNs of both signs, infinities, signed zeros, denormals, +-65504, +-128) are bit-identical to E460. Clean rows agree with NumPy's softmax to 0.09% (the f16 result). exp-below-max.ll @softmax_f16. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E462 | **Adopted: a deferred running maximum of extended halves kept in half** (deferredMinMaxReductions, -agx-deferred-minmax-half). When every value of the chain is fpext of a half, the initial value is a constant exact in half and no in-loop use reads the running value, the deferred chain runs on the halves. The m, nsz maximum, z and i16 bits are as E453. Its result is extended once on the exit edge. Exact: the maximum of extensions is the extension of the maximum, NaN payloads included, and f16 compares flush no denormal, so the half form has none of the float form's zero / denormal corner. softmax_f16 (E461's kernel): the first loop's per-element conversion goes (load, fcmpsel ltn, icmpsel sgt on 16-bit registers). tput 721.5 -> 712.0, cycles 12048.5 -> 11984.5. No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: E461's softmax_f16 data set and 64 rows of all-negative halves are bit-identical to E461. Sabotage (an initial value not exact in half accepted), on a variant whose maximum starts at 0.1: 6 differences on the all-negative rows. agx-loop-fuzz has no half maxima (0 of 300 differ). deferred-minmax.ll @halves. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E463 | **Adopted: maximum's deferred value also for its zero-sign-free uses in the loop** (deferredMinMaxReductions, -agx-deferred-minmax-side-uses). E446 let a running maximumnum's in-loop uses that ZeroSignFree accepts (an online softmax's e^(m - m'), e^(v - m')) take the deferred value. maximum (MLIR's arith.maximumf) kept its full expansion there. Since E453 its deferred value, the nsz maximum, carries the NaN maximum gives (the same payload). It differs only in a zero's sign, or a denormal where maximum gives the zero a compare flushes it to, which such uses do not see. So those uses take it too. New MLIR bench kernel online_softmax (one-pass log-sum-exp of 1024-float rows by a SIMD-group, merged across lanes; README row). Its loop's maximum goes from and, fcmpsel ltn, fmul, fcmpsel eq to fcmpsel ltn, icmpsel sgt. tput 679.7 -> 652.9 (cycles 4998.8 -> 4865.5, GPRs 25 -> 27, 55 groups either way). No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: 6 seeds of 128 rows (normals, sprinkled specials, special-only, 30% specials, zero / denormal-heavy rows of -1, zero / denormal-only rows) are bit-identical to E462, and within 6.4e-8 of NumPy's log-sum-exp. Sabotage (the in-loop uses one link late) gives 302 differences. agx-loop-fuzz differs on 11 of 400 seeds. deferred-minmax.ll @online_maximum. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E464 | **Adopted: an online softmax's two exponentials as one** (AGXCodeGenPrepare onlineSoftmaxExps, -agx-online-softmax-exp, after the deferred maxima and before the expansions). e^(a - u) and e^(b - u) with u = maximum(a, b) or maximumnum (nsz or not, deferred or not), precise exps in one block: the rescale e^(m - m') and new term e^(v - m'). Where b > a, u is b and b - u = b - b is +0, or a NaN for an infinite b. Its exponential is 1 or a NaN, which 1 + (b - u) gives exactly (the expansion's e^0 is 1.0; the simulator's arithmetic NaNs are its default one). Otherwise (b <= a, a tie of zeros, or a NaN) a - u is the trivial one: u is a, the other zero, or a NaN. So x = b > a ? a - u : b - u, e = e^x, e^(a - u) = b > a ? e : 1 + (a - u), e^(b - u) = b > a ? 1 + (b - u) : e. One exponential (about 20 instructions) for two, plus an fcmpsel, two fadds and two selects. x is at most 0 or a NaN, so the new exp carries !agx.exp.below.max (its upper clamp and +inf select go, as the originals' did). online_softmax (E463's kernel) tput 652.9 -> 465.4, cycles 4865.5 -> 4531.7, 158 -> 147 instructions, GPRs 27 -> 25. No other kernel changes (the CUDA online softmaxes use the approximate exp2, left alone). Verified on agx_run: online_softmax's E463 data set is bit-identical to E463. A kernel storing both exponentials for maximum / maximumnum, nsz or not, both operand orders, over every pair of 22 specials (zeros, denormals, infinities, NaNs with payloads, exp's clamp bounds, the largest floats) plus 5000 equal pairs and random pairs, is bit-identical (4 -> 2 exps after CSE). Sabotage (the first result's select arms swapped) gives 442 differences. online-softmax-exp.ll. Also measured on a new bench kernel, online_softmax_vec4 (a thread per row in vector<4xf32> steps; its exps are scalars by this pass): tput 20231.2 -> 12930.6, cycles 71473.3 -> 68718.2, 831 -> 641 instructions, GPRs 56 -> 49 (28 -> 32 groups). Bit-identical to E463 on the same data set, within 9.4e-8 of NumPy. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E465 | **Adopted: a float division of converted integers without exponent scaling** (AGXISelLowering lowerFDIV32, -agx-fdiv-int-quotient). Both operands are sitofp / uitofp of at most 32 bits, or sums / differences of such to depth 2: integer-valued floats of magnitude 0 or [1, 2^34], never -0, an infinity or a NaN. 1 / b is a normal float and every nonzero quotient is at least 2^-34, so y (rcp and E282's step, correctly rounded), q0 = a y, r = a - b q0 (exact) and q = q0 + r y give the correctly rounded quotient. That is rcp, five fmas / fmuls and one select instead of the general expansion's bfi scalings, exponent ands and two more multiplies. For b = 0 the step is a NaN (-0 * inf + 1); a * rcp(0) = a * inf is the quotient there (+-inf, 0 / 0 a NaN), selected on b == 0. A first version selected q0, already a NaN: 1889 of 4194304 test quotients differed, all at b = 0. rope's (2i) / dim: tput 43.0 -> 40.9, cycles 269.1 -> 264.3, bytes 602 -> 570. No other kernel changes. Verified on agx_run: 2^20 operand pairs (every pair of 25 edge integers: 0, +-1, 2^24 +- 1, INT_MIN / MAX, ...; random full-range, small, 24-bit by small divisors, full-range by +-300) in four forms (sitofp, uitofp, a sum numerator, a difference divisor) are bit-identical to E464. The sitofp quotients with b != 0 also equal NumPy's correctly rounded ones. Sabotage (no correction step, q = q0) gives 281422 differences on 2^18 random pairs. fdiv-int-quotient.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E466 | **Adopted: E464 also for maxnum** (onlineSoftmaxExps). llvm.maxnum (C's fmaxf, MLIR's maxnumf) returns an operand and drops one NaN, as maximumnum does. So where b > a, u is b; otherwise u is a, or b where a is a NaN. There the trivial side's 1 + (x - u) is 1 + NaN, a NaN, as e^NaN is. New MLIR bench kernel online_softmax_maxnum (online_softmax with maxnumf; README row): tput 635.5 -> 448.3, cycles 4841.6 -> 4508.9. No other kernel changes (the CUDA online softmaxes use the approximate exp2). Verified on agx_run: the kernel on E463's data set is bit-identical to E465. The pair kernel with maxnum variants is too (every pair of 23 specials, a signalling NaN included, 5000 equal pairs, random; 2 -> 1 exps). online-softmax-exp.ll @pair_maxnum. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E467 | **Fixed: byteSources lost the or of a value narrower than a byte** (AGXCodeGenPrepare permuteBytes' byteSources, from 5566ba54e, 2026-11-04). A deep agx-cf-fuzz run (seeds 6000-7000) failed seed 6210 with every compiler of this session (E452 onward), at llc -O0 as at -O3 after opt -O1 / -O2. Localized: opt-bisect showed instcombine's output first failing. alive2 passed that step, and lli's interpreter on a driver matched the fuzzer's interpreter for both IRs, so the backend was miscompiling. llvm-reduce, with agx_run against lli as the test, reduced it to (load(p) & 255) | zext(i1 true) stored: the or was gone. byteSources' zext case took any source of at most 16 bits as a leaf, and Leaf gives a value of b bits b / 8 bytes: an i1 none, all zero, so the or of disjoint bytes kept only the masked byte. Now only an i8 or i16 source is a leaf of bytes; a narrower one leaves the zext opaque (its four bytes). No bench kernel changes. Seed 6210 passes, as does a cf run of seeds 6000-7500. byte-permute-zext-i1.ll (-O0 and -O3; it fails with the previous compiler). | fixed | -- |

| L15-E469 | **Adopted: the precise exponential's n = round(x / ln 2) as t + 1.5 2^23 - 1.5 2^23, its integer the sum's bits** (AGXMathExpansion expHiLo, `-agx-exp-magic-round`; exp, exp2, exp10, pow and the hyperbolic functions through it): the rint (on the shared transcendental unit) and the float-to-integer convert (on the shared multiply / bit-field unit, two of each per core: the simulator's SHARED_UNITS) become two fadd32s on each scheduler's FP pipe. t = x log2(e) is clamped to |t| <= 151, so t + 1.5 2^23 is in [2^23, 2^24), where the ulp is 1: the sum rounds t to the nearest integer, ties to even as rint does (1.5 2^23 is even), and n = sum - 1.5 2^23 is exact; the sum's bits are 0x4b400000 + n, and 0x4b400000 << 23 is 0 mod 2^32, so the scale's exponent addition bits(e^r) + (n << 23) takes them unconverted (E457's imadd with 2^23 now also for a bitcast float; the non-flushing two-multiplication scale would take bits - 0x4b400000, an integer subtraction, but no f32 expansion runs there today). The subtraction's -1.5 2^23 took a second register (+2 GPRs: attn_decode_f16 52 -> 54, 32 -> 30 groups, tput 9505.2 -> 9717.1); a negative float constant source whose magnitude the same block also uses is now that register with the neg modifier (AGXISelDAGToDAG SelectFPSrc, `-agx-neg-constant-modifiers`). Every negative constant that way (each alone too) was worse: online_softmax 465.4 -> 471.7, online_softmax_vec4 12930.6 -> 12982.0. MLIR bench, tput: online_softmax_vec4 12930.6 -> 12706.7 (-1.7%), softmax_f16 712.0 -> 686.6 (-3.6%), causal_rowmax 394.2 -> 379.4 (-3.8%), xent_rows 382.6 -> 372.7 (-2.6%), softmax_rows 181.5 -> 175.6 (-3.3%), online_softmax 465.4 -> 460.1, online_softmax_maxnum 448.3 -> 444.7, silu_f16x4 80.4 -> 79.8; attn_decode_f16 9505.2 -> 9600.1 (+1.0%: 52 -> 53 GPRs, 32 -> 30 groups; the magic constant's register); newk gelu_tanh 23.0 the same; agx-bench (measured with E473: the first runs, with --llc outside the build directory and no --objcopy, failed to start): cross_entropy tput 701.6 -> 673.3 (-4.0%), cycles 13372.9 -> 13316.4, 21 -> 22 GPRs, 770 -> 776 bytes; softmax_row cycles 238.5 -> 239.7, 354 -> 360 bytes (tput the same); the rest unchanged (ex2.approx). agx_run: exp, exp2 and exp10 of all 2^32 floats bit-identical to the E467 compiler (tanh, sinh and cosh: E470); online_softmax, online_softmax_maxnum and online_softmax_vec4 (1024 x 1024, scales 0.1 to 200, -inf and NaN elements: the exponentials below the loop maximum, without the overflow clamp) bit-identical; a sabotaged magic of 2^23 differs on all 8,388,608 floats of [-1, -0.5); fuzzers 0 failing. | L15-E472 | **Adopted: no NaN select after an exponential without the +inf select** (AGXMathExpansion ldexp, `-agx-exp-nan-through-scale`, without the canonical-nan feature; E469's scale): the scale bits(e^r) + (n << 23) ended in select(e^r is NaN, e^r, scaled) because a NaN argument made n arbitrary. With PTX's canonical NaN (0x7fffffff, low bits set) the select stays. | L15-E476 | **Adopted: a loop whose reductions take their identity once a causal mask holds ends there** (AGXCodeGenPrepare maskedTailExits, `-agx-masked-tail-exits`; after the indices are rebased and narrowed). A single-block loop with only loads for side effects, an induction iv (iv.next = iv + s nuw, exit iv.next < UB unsigned), and every other loop-carried value a reduction P' = op(P, select(m, identity, x)) or P' = select(m, P, y), all with one mask m = A > K or A >= K, A = iv or iv + c (nuw), c and K invariant: once m holds it holds for the rest of the loop, whose iterations then change nothing. UB becomes umin(UB, max(K - c, 0) (+ 1 for >)), the subtraction in the narrower type where K and c are zero extensions (the + 1 saturating in the wide one), computed before the loop: the exit stays one compare of the induction. Identities: fadd's -0, or +0 where the start is never -0 (then no sum is); maxnum / minnum's -inf / +inf where the start is no NaN (then no result is); maximum / minimum's always; add, or, xor's 0. Only the reductions' values are used after the loop. A first version that added the mask of the next iteration to the exit condition (an and, then a logical and) kept the loop's per-iteration work but lost the indices' rebasing to 32 bits (the exit no longer one compare): causal_rowmax tput 368.8 -> 413.6 / 436.4 although 35% fewer cycles. Tests: masked-tail-exits.ll (with a store in the loop, a sum from -0, maxnum from a NaN, a value used after the loop, a mask turning off: none), rebase-compares.ll (run without it). | adopted | -- |

| L15-E479 | **Adopted: an exponential below a maximum whose result only reaches flushing arithmetic is clamped at its +0 bound, without the +0 select** (AGXMathExpansion expHiLo, `-agx-exp-flushed-underflow`; AGXCodeGenPrepare onlyFlushingUses). Below-the-maximum exps (E450, softmax) kept the clamp at -104 and the select of +0 for scaled bits below 2^23. At E474's exact bound c = -87.3365478515625 the scaled bits are 0x007fffxx, a positive denormal's; clamped there (a NaN fails the compare and stays one), every argument at or below it gives that denormal, which a user that flushes a denormal operand reads as the +0 the select gave. So where every use, through selects' values, phis, negations and vector element moves, is an f32 fadd / fsub / fmul / fma (flushing f32; none with a +-0 or +-1 constant operand, which a fold could turn into the unflushed operand itself), the select goes: one instruction per exp in the softmax loops. MLIR bench, tput: online_softmax_vec4 11840.0 -> 11558.4 (-2.4%), attn_decode_f16 9453.8 -> 9319.0 (-1.4%), xent_rows 358.4 -> 343.8 (-4.1%), causal_rowmax 229.2 -> 219.6 (-4.2%), softmax_rows 170.6 -> 167.2, online_softmax 438.0 -> 429.9, online_softmax_maxnum 437.4 -> 424.2 (-3.0%), softmax_f16 643.5 -> 628.0 (-2.4%); agx-bench cross_entropy tput 653.8 -> 636.6 (-2.6%), 768 -> 760 bytes (baseline updated). agx_run against the E478 compiler: an exponential marked below a maximum feeding x + y, x * y, fma(x, y, z) and y + select(c, x, z) (y, z cycling through +-0, +-1, +-denormals, +-inf, NaN, ...) over every float from -0 to the negative NaNs, every positive NaN and +0 up to 2^22: identical; online_softmax, online_softmax_maxnum, online_softmax_vec4, xent_rows, softmax_f16 (E469 data and E477's signed-zero rows), softmax_rows, causal_rowmax and attn_decode_f16 (heads with scores scaled by 40 and 200, infinite keys, NaN values, -0 keys; buffers at offset 0 and 2) identical; a clamp one float above the bound differs on 1,206,507 words of [-112, -80]; deep fuzz seeds 7000+ (cf 1000, loop 800, mem 400, ...) 0 failing. (Without the constant-operand guard x + -0.0 was not folded on this target either: the guard is kept as a precaution.) Tests: exp-magic-round.ll (below_max_sum), online-softmax-exp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E480 | **Adopted: E476 for masks the tail compare implies** (AGXCodeGenPrepare maskedTailExits, `-agx-masked-tail-or`): a reduction's mask may be an or of the tail compare (A > K, A >= K) with anything, or, where the select keeps x under its condition and the identity otherwise, that condition an and of the inverse compare (A <= K, A < K) with anything: once the tail compare holds, the mask does. A sliding window's j <= row && j + w >= row is the second (InstCombine leaves select(and(..), x, -inf)); an and of the tail compare with something else is no mask the compare implies and is left. All of one loop's reductions must share the tail compare (A, K, predicate). New MLIR bench kernel window_rowmax (causal_rowmax with a window of 128): tput 501.0 -> 300.7 (-40.0%), cycles 6968.0 -> 4179.7; nothing else in the MLIR bench, newk or agx-bench changed (the iterations below row - 128 still run: raising the start is left). agx_run: window_rowmax over all rows (E469 data, signed-zero rows) identical to the E479 compiler; 12 generated loops (steps 1, 3, 32; > and >=; the or-masked and the and-unmasked forms; six reductions each) over K, K2, c, UB combinations (38,808 threads) identical to -agx-masked-tail-or=0; with the wrong shapes (and-masked, or-unmasked) the compiler leaves the loops alone, and a sabotage accepting them differs in 9,936 words; fuzzers loop 400, cf 600 seeds, 0 failing. Test: masked-tail-exits.ll (window, masked_and). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E481 | **Adopted: E476's loops also start where a mask true up to some iteration lets go** (AGXCodeGenPrepare maskedTailExits / raiseMaskedHead, `-agx-masked-head-starts`; AGXNarrowInductions upperBound): a head mask A < K (A <= K), A an increasing recurrence with the induction's step, implied by every reduction's mask like E480's tail compare (a sliding window's j + w >= row, unmasked), and one induction in the loop: its start S0 becomes S0 + s min(ceil(max(K - A0, 0) / s), k_max) (K + 1 for <=), k_max = max(UB - (S0 + 1), 0) / s the last iteration that ran (where every iteration is masked, the last runs, as an identity: the loads stay those of iterations that ran), applied after the tail's bound; written umin(start, umax(UB, S0)), the same value, so that SCEV and upperBound bound it. upperBound bounds an increasing recurrence also by its loop's exit, the start or below X where the latch goes on while the next value is below X (SCEV's count of the raised loop was 2^27), and an unsigned maximum by its operands'. A version shifting the induction's uses by d with the bound UB - d instead (the start kept) left even the induction 64-bit (160.6 tput). The masks' adds stay 64-bit (the indices' rebasing to 32 bits does not take the raised start). MLIR bench window_rowmax: cycles 4179.7 -> 1303.3, tput 300.7 -> 136.2 (-54.7%), 80 -> 125 instructions, 28 -> 32 GPRs (55 -> 52 groups); nothing else in the MLIR bench, newk or agx-bench changed. agx_run: window_rowmax (all rows, E469 data, signed-zero rows) and causal_rowmax identical to the E480 compiler; E480's 12 window loops and 6 new head-only loops (the masked A < K2 and the unmasked A >= K2 forms; steps 1, 3, 32) over the K, K2, c, UB combinations (58,212 threads) and E476's 12 tail loops (26,136) identical to -agx-masked-tail-exits=0; a sabotage rounding the steps skipped up by one more differs in 3,585 words; fuzzers loop 400, cf 600, mem 300 seeds, 0 failing. Test: masked-tail-exits.ll (window, its start). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E482 | **Adopted: AGXNarrowInductions' upper bounds fall back to SCEV's unsigned range** (upperBound, `-agx-upper-bound-unknown-ranges`): its structural bound (constants, positions, sums and products of bounded terms, zero extensions, recurrences by their trip counts) had none for a sum with a negative term or a value SCEV leaves unknown, and E481's raised start, umin(s + k t, umax(n, s)) with n = min(1024 - lane, ...), is both: the window loop's counter got the bound 2^32 - 1 (its truncation's), so its index arithmetic and masks stayed 64-bit. Where the structural bound has none, SCEV's unsigned range maximum (sound; the special registers' ranges are known by then) is taken. MLIR bench window_rowmax: tput 136.2 -> 110.0 (-19.2%; 300.7 before E481), 125 -> 114 instructions, 32 -> 28 GPRs (52 -> 55 groups), its loops 32-bit like causal_rowmax's; nothing else in the MLIR bench, newk or agx-bench changed. agx_run: window_rowmax and causal_rowmax identical to the E481 compiler; the 44 generated loops of E476, E480 and E481 (84,348 threads) identical; fuzzers loop 1,200 seeds (0:600, 1500:2100), cf 600, mem 300, int 300, 0 failing; a sabotage taking a 256th of the range as the bound fails 65 of 150 loop-fuzz seeds. Test: narrow-raised-start.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E483 | **Adopted: silu's x / (e^-x + 1) as a scaled Markstein quotient step** (AGXCodeGenPrepare reciprocalsAboveOne, `-agx-silu-quotient`; precise exp, no fast-math flags, the numerator the exponent's own negated operand): d = e^-x + 1 is in [1, +inf] (or a NaN), so instead of the general division's scalings of both operands, its rcp, fmas and special-case selects: where d > 2^64 both d and x times 2^-64 (x < -44 there: exact), E282's correctly rounded y = 1 / d, q0 = x y and Markstein's correction with the residual negated, r' = d q0 - x, q = q0 - r' y (bit for bit the same where x is not zero, and -0 for x = -0, where x - d q0 = +0 lost the sign: a first version differed on all 8,388,608 floats from -0 to the negative denormals), y as 0 at d = +inf (q0 = x * 0, the quotient: -0, a NaN for x = -inf) and q0 where the residual is a NaN (x = +inf: a version without differed there). The quotient of one value: agx_run gives the old compiler's bits for every float x (2^32, sweeps against the E482 compiler), and silu_f16x4 over every half. MLIR bench silu_f16x4: tput 72.8 -> 69.1 (-5.1%), 177 -> 163 instructions, 27 -> 25 GPRs; nothing else in the MLIR bench, newk or agx-bench changed (the CUDA kernels' silu takes rcp.approx). Fuzzers fp and half 200 seeds each, 0 failing. Test: silu-quotient.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E484 | **Adopted: precise 2^x and 10^x below a maximum as exp is** (AGXCodeGenPrepare `-agx-other-base-exps`, AGXMathExpansion `-agx-exp2-below-max`): E464's one-exponential online softmax pair and the below-maximum analyses (expBelowLoopMax, !agx.exp.below.max, noExpOverflow's a - max(a, b)) took only exp. b^x is 1 at x = +-0 and a NaN at a NaN as e^x is, so the pair rewrite holds for two exp2s or two exp10s (not mixed bases). Below a maximum x is at most 0, a flushed denormal or a NaN, so the clamp at 128 / 38.6 never fires and goes. Also hi = x ln b <= 0 gives n <= 0 and a scaled e^r below 2: no +inf select, and (native NaNs, E472) no NaN select, since a NaN x's magic bits shift to 0 and leave e^r's NaN. New MLIR bench online_softmax_exp2 (online_softmax with math.exp2, as attention kernels with log2(e) in their scale): tput 861.9 -> 502.7, cycles 5644.2 -> 4642.9, 173 -> 153 instructions, GPRs 31 -> 28 (loop 56 -> 35 instructions). No other MLIR bench or agx-bench kernel changes (the CUDA kernels' exp2s are approximate). Verified on agx_run against E483: 2^x and 10^x with !agx.exp.below.max and of a negated argument, over every float x <= 0 (the negatives, +0, the positive denormals, the NaNs), 0 results differ; a sabotaged lower clamp (-120) differs in 8,388,607 results; online_softmax_exp2 with maximumf / maxnumf, data with -inf, NaNs and signed-zero rows, bit-identical; online_softmax unchanged. lit online-softmax-exp2.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E485 | **Adopted: precise 2^x and 10^x with +inf and +0 by compares of x, no clamps** (AGXMathExpansion boundsByArg, `-agx-exp2-bounds-by-arg`; E473 / E474 for exp, here with the low part of x ln b): x was clamped to [-151, 128] / [-46, 38.6] and the scaled word selected +inf (sge 0x7f800000) and +0 (slt 0x00800000), then a NaN select. Over every float, E484's 2^x is +inf exactly from 128 and +0 exactly at or below -126 - 2^-17 (0xc2fc0001), each a contiguous run up to the infinities; 10^x is +inf from 38.5318413 (0x421a209b) and +0 at or below -37.9297829 (0xc217b819). So: +inf where x >= the first, +0 where x <= the second, and no clamps; n, r and the integer sum are garbage beyond the bounds (an infinity's a NaN, the sum wrapping), all selected away, with no poison (the magic bits, no conversion, no nsw). No NaN select: a NaN x fails both compares, its magic bits shift to 0 and leave e^r's NaN (native NaNs; the magic sum and integer scale required, else the clamps stay). For x = -y the compares are of y, mirrored. Below a maximum (E484) only the +0 compare. A lone 2^x / 10^x 51 -> 47 instructions (2^x below a maximum 45 -> 44); fp.ll's transcendental 5 selects -> 2, GPRs 31 -> 30. MLIR bench online_softmax_exp2 tput 502.7 -> 493.8, cycles 4642.9 -> 4571.3, 153 -> 148 instructions; nothing else changes (agx-bench and the other MLIR kernels have no precise 2^x / 10^x). Verified on agx_run against E484: 2^x, 10^x, 2^-y and 10^-y over all 2^32 inputs, and their below-maximum forms over every float x <= 0: 0 results differ; a +0 bound one ulp off (-126) differs in 1 result (x = -126); online_softmax_exp2 (maximumf / maxnumf, -inf, NaN and signed-zero data) bit-identical. lit exp2-bounded.ll (CLAMP: the flag off), online-softmax-exp2.ll, fp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E486 | **Adopted: sinh / cosh's e^a / 2 with +inf by a compare of a** (AGXMathExpansion halfExp, `-agx-half-exp-overflow-by-arg`; E485's boundsByArg): halfExp (a = |x| >= 0: e^a / 2, or e^(a - ln 2) from 88 with ln 2 in two parts) clamped hi at 89, selected +inf on the scaled word (icmpsel sgt), then a NaN select. Over every float, E485's sinh and cosh are +inf exactly from |x| = 89.4159927 (0x42b2d4fd). So +inf where a >= that, no clamp (hi, n and the integer sum beyond it are garbage the select replaces; no poison), and no NaN select (a NaN a fails the compare; its magic bits shift to 0 and leave e^r's NaN; native NaNs only, as E485). sinh 70 -> 67 instructions, cosh 60 -> 57; the bfloat forms likewise (half uses the hardware's exp2). No agx-bench or MLIR bench kernel has sinh / cosh (unchanged). Verified on agx_run against E485: sinh and cosh over all 2^32 floats, 0 results differ; bfloat sinh / cosh over all 65,536, 0 differ; the threshold one ulp low (0x42b2d4fc) differs in 1 result (cosh there 0x7f7fffec). tanh, exp, exp2, sin and the half forms compile identically. lit tanh-bounded-exp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E487 | **Adopted: vectors of bytes in alignment versioning, their lanes sign-extended in the 16-bit halves** (AGXISelLowering `-agx-signed-bytes-half-lanes`, AGXAlignmentVersioning `-agx-alignment-versioning-byte-vectors`): MLIR's vector<4xi8> loads are align 1, so dot_i8 (new MLIR bench: int8 GEMV, a thread per row) loaded each byte apart, each sign-extended as (b ^ 128) - 128: per byte a load, xor and isub. Versioning byte loops was rejected earlier (a 4-byte load leaves each byte in a 16-bit half, which the sign extension copied to 32 bits first: three instructions per byte again, more GPRs). Now the i32 sign_extend_inreg of an extended 16-bit lane with a zero high byte, whose users are all add / sub / mul, is (y ^ 128) - 128 on the 16-bit half and a sign_extend, which imadd's 16-bit sources fold (imadd r, a.sx, b.sx, acc): two instructions per byte, no copy. (Not for other users: a shift left folded the - 128 into its iadd's addend, 26 -> 29 instructions.) Then vector-of-byte accesses count for versioning (scalar bytes still not: q8_matvec's converted bytes 201.9 -> 217.8 tput, q4_matvec's 289.4 -> 293.2 when they did). dot_i8 tput 2788.6 -> 2239.0 (-19.7%), cycles 41845.0 -> 21288.2, GPRs 36 -> 38, groups 46 -> 42 (149 -> 261 instructions: two loop copies). Micro-kernels of <2 / 4 x i8> loads sign-extended into add / mul / mul-add / mul by a constant: 2-8 instructions fewer; ashr, shl, compare, sdiv, xor, sitofp unchanged. agx-bench and the other MLIR kernels unchanged. Verified on agx_run against E486: dot_i8 on random, extreme (-128 / 127) and all-bytes data with the matrix / vector at byte offsets 0-3 (both copies), bit-identical and equal to a numpy reference; 22 micro-kernels (11 users x 2 / 4 lanes) over every pair of bytes, identical; a sabotaged bias (xor 127) differs in 10 micro-kernels and dot_i8's aligned case. lit alignment-versioning.ll (dot_i8; NOLANE: the copy). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E488 | **Adopted: two byte lanes of a vector load biased by one 32-bit xor** (AGXISelLowering, E487's sign extension of a lane, `-agx-signed-byte-pairs`): a byte vector load (LOAD_V2_I8 / LOAD_V4_I8) puts lanes 2k and 2k + 1 in the halves of one 32-bit register, so their (y ^ 128) takes one xor of the register with 0x00800080 (a hoisted mov_imm; opaque, so that the truncations to the halves do not narrow it back to two xors), then the 16-bit isub of each half. Per 4 bytes 2 xors and 4 isubs instead of 4 and 4. dot_i8 tput 2239.0 -> 1958.1 (-12.5%), cycles 21288.2 -> 20161.8, 261 -> 246 instructions, GPRs 38 (unchanged). E487's micro-kernels: add / sub / mul / mul-add 1-3 instructions fewer; <3 x i8> and <8 x i8> forms 1-23 fewer (mul8 86 -> 63 against E486). agx-bench and the other MLIR kernels unchanged. Verified on agx_run against E487: dot_i8 on random, extreme and all-bytes data at byte offsets 0-3, bit-identical and equal to a numpy reference; the 22 micro-kernels over every pair of bytes and <3 / 8 x i8> add / mul against E486, identical; a sabotaged bias (0x00800081) differs in 10 micro-kernels and dot_i8's aligned case. lit alignment-versioning.ll (NOPAIR: the option off). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E489 | **Rejected: a branch on f || p (or f && p), f a float compare with a constant, as f's compare of select(p, k, x)** (AGXISelLowering IF / LOOP combine, prototype `-agx-branch-selected-compare`; k a constant for which x cc c holds, or fails for &&: c itself, a NaN for an unordered compare, +-inf, 0). mandelbrot's merged loop exit select(|z|^2 uge 4, 1, i == max) was an icmpsel (i == max ? 1 : 0), an fcmpsel and the branch's compare with zero; as select(i == max, 4.0, |z|^2) uge 4 it is one icmpsel and the branch's own fcmp (AGXFoldOperands): loop 10 -> 9 instructions, cycles 12315.9 -> 12065.9 (-2%).
