No other agx-bench or MLIR kernel has the form. | rejected | tput worse |
| L15-E490 | **Adopted: a loop's chain of selects of the counter plus constants kept relative to the counter** (AGXCodeGenPrepare relativeIndexSelects, `-agx-relative-index-selects`): an unrolled argmax carries its index p through select(c_k, i + k, p) for k = 0..s-1 (i the counter, MLIR's trunc(i | k)): every copy computes its i + k (an or or add; a 64-bit add for an i64 index), only for the select. As d = p - i (a header phi from p0 - i0): the selects take the constants k (immediates of fcmpsel / icmpsel), d - s once an iteration at the latch, and p = (d - s) + i_next where it is used after the loop, with i_next as SCEV's exit value expanded after the loop where it has one (with the counter itself, strength reduction, which addresses by byte offsets, kept another 32-bit counter just for it). Exact in wrapping arithmetic. Applies to a header phi whose latch value is a chain of at least two selects in the header, each the previous link or p on one side and the counter plus a constant (SCEV) on the other, at least two nonzero constants, p and each link used only by the next link, the last only by p and after the loop where the exit block (single, its one predecessor the latch) dominates the use (for a phi, its edge: a first try replaced a runtime-unrolled loop's remainder phi in another block, which the verifier caught). MLIR bench argmax_rows tput 437.1 -> 342.8 (-21.6%), cycles 6419.0 -> 6208.8, 30 -> 29 instructions (loop 16 -> 14); agx-bench unchanged (cuda_argmax is not unrolled: its select takes the counter itself). Unrolled (by 3 / 4, runtime trip counts with remainder loops) argmax / argmin / last-maximum / decrementing / offset (i + 1000) / i64-index / two-chain loops: main loops 1-3 instructions fewer (i64: three 64-bit adds for one); a scaled index (2i) and an index also used in the loop untouched. Verified on agx_run against E488: argmax_rows on rows of -inf, NaNs, ties, constants, signed zeros, +inf and extreme values, bit-identical and equal to a reference; the 27 IR variants (9 kinds, not unrolled and unrolled by 3 and 4) over 13 (n, start) pairs (empty, 1-5 elements, odd counts, a start offset), identical; a sabotaged step (+s) differs in 22 of 52 runs and in argmax_rows. lit relative-index-selects.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E491 | **Adopted: ranged compares with the positions' upper bounds and known bits** (AGXCodeGenPrepare rangedCompares, `-agx-ranged-compares-bounds`): rangedCompares (E300) decided compares from ranges of the special registers, but without MLIR's upper bounds (E391 keeps them out of instruction selection), and computeConstantRange gives no range for an or of two values. conv3x3_pad's centre tap is guarded by (gx | gy) <u 1024 with gx, gy < 1024 (upper_bound): always true, kept as an if / pop and a zero select. Now the ranges are added as for an analysis (with the upper bounds and the grid's, taken off again) and a compare is also decided by the operands' known-bits ranges (the or: nothing at or above bit 10). The known bits only decide; the rebasing of x + k < c as x < c - k keeps the plain ranges (with known bits it turned a loop's i + 4 < 256 into i < 252, which kept E431's two congruent counters apart). MLIR bench conv3x3_pad tput 37.1 -> 35.3 (-4.9%), 90 -> 86 instructions, cycles 572.3 -> 568.6, GPRs 22 -> 23 (72 groups both); nothing else changes. Also measured, not kept: a guard-free copy of a chain of 4 or more guarded straight-line loads (AGXAlignmentVersioning prototype, the conditions hoisted to the start under a SIMD-group ballot, as versionGuards for loops): on an inner tile conv3x3_pad cycles 572.3 -> 352.7 and tput 37.8 -> 32.6, but the bench's tile 0 (the image's corner, every tile of its first row at the border) 37.1 -> 46.6: the hoisted conditions are merged with the guarded copy's own and held through it (22 -> 33 GPRs); freezing their leaves to keep them apart was worse (155 instructions, 56.8). Verified on agx_run against E490: conv3x3_pad over the whole 1024 x 1024 grid (every border tile; NaNs, infinities, signed zeros, extreme magnitudes) bit-identical; a sabotaged range ([0, 1023) for every value) folds a border tap's guard and reads unmapped memory. lit ranged-compares-bounds.ll; narrow-signed-compares.ll (x - 1 > -1 now x >u 0 with the bounds). | adopted | -- |

| L15-E496 | **Adopted: zext(x) + c <u k as the 32-bit x <u k - c where x + c stays in [0, 2^32)** (AGXNarrowInductions narrowOffsetCompares, `-agx-narrow-offset-compares`; unsigned and equality compares, x an i32, c and k constants, x's range from SCEV, with the pass's final analyses): loop strength reduction writes an unrolled loop's exit test (i | 12) <u 1020 as zext(i') - 4 <u 1020 on i' = i + 16, which the narrowing of the counter leaves as a 64-bit subtraction and compare: an isub of a pair, three selects and the branch on their result, where i' <u 1024 is the branch's own while_icmp. (E447's rebasing and E300's rangedCompares run before strength reduction makes it.) MLIR bench dot_i8 tput 1958.1 -> 1745.3 (-10.9%), 246 -> 237 instructions, GPRs 38 -> 37 (42 -> 44 groups); new MLIR bench gemv_q8f (int8 weights converted to f32, vector<4xi8>) 1573.1 -> 1424.3 (-9.5%), 156 -> 147; agx-bench and the other MLIR kernels unchanged. Verified on agx_run against E491: dot_i8 at byte offsets 0-3 (random, extreme, all-bytes data), bit-identical and equal to a reference; gemv_q8f with NaN / -inf activations at offsets 0-8, identical; eight dot_i8-shaped kernels (bounds 8, 16, 36, 512 from 4, 1020, 1024, runtime min(n, 1020) from 0 and 8) over 13 n each, aligned and not, identical (the fold in two of them); a sabotaged bound (k - c + 4) reads unmapped memory. lit narrow-offset-compares.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E497 | **Measured, not kept: exp below a vector loop's lane-wise maximum** (AGXCodeGenPrepare expBelowLoopMax prototype `-agx-exp-below-vector-max`): a new kernel, softmax_f16v4 (a thread per 1024-half row in vector<4xf16> steps: lane-wise maximumf, vector.reduction, exp of x - splat(m)), keeps every exponential's +inf select, as its max is a reduction of four lane maxima. Accepting x - splat(m) with m a maximum chain over all lanes of the loop's vector maximum (NaN-dropping chains only over NaN-dropping lane maxima) still finds nothing: the maximum loop is unrolled by 4 (its maximum a chain within the iteration, not max(phi, load)) and the exponential loops differently, so the per-iteration address match fails. Reverted; the kernel is not in the bench. | measured | -- |
| L15-E498 | **Adopted: E494's loads-first original order, kept only where the registers still let the kernel's own threadgroups in** (AGXTargetMachine AGXScheduleDAGMILive / AGXOriginalOrderStrategy, `-agx-sched-original-order-loads-first`): a region E239 reverts to its original order, without barriers or threadgroup accesses, takes its ready device loads first; that order is dropped for the plain one where its halves (plus 16 for register allocation) lower the threads below the kernel's known maximum per threadgroup (agx-max-threads-per-threadgroup, else 1024) or cut the core's SIMD-groups 1.8 times (E239's tests). (E436 and E493 (b), without these limits, made the threadgroup-tiled gemm_smem_tile and matrixMul worse.) E494 used the threads before the region's schedule as the bound, which reverted attention decode (whose threadgroups are 32 threads); q4_matvec (no known size), which E494 took to 54 registers and 896 threads, stays at 24 and 1024 (its loads-first region, 39 -> 100 halves, cuts the core's groups 82 -> 32). MLIR bench attn_decode_f16 tput 9319.0 -> 8864.8 (-4.9%), cycles 114002.6 -> 74066.6, GPRs 53 -> 77 (still 640 threads for its 32); online_softmax_vec4 11558.4 -> 11548.5 (58 GPRs, 832 threads for its 256); softmax_f16v4 (not in the bench) 18562.3 -> 18479.9; agx-bench and the other MLIR kernels unchanged, no kernel's thread limit changed. Verified on agx_run against E496: attn_decode_f16, online_softmax_vec4 (with signed-zero rows), softmax_rows bit-identical (an order of the same dependence graph). lit sched-loads-first.ll (77 vs 53 registers), sched-loads-first-threads.ll (q4_matvec at 1024 threads). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E499 | **Adopted: f32 erf's rational approximation divided by the reciprocal, a Newton step and a residual correction** (GPUToAIR, `erf-reciprocal-division`): upstream's erf (P / Q of |x| per interval, 1 beyond 3.75) took the general precise division (exponent scaling of both operands, the reciprocal, the corrections, the special-case selects: some 20 instructions). Now GPUToAIR expands math.erf itself (upstream's patterns, f16 / bf16 widened as before), noting the division they create through a rewrite listener (upstream's pattern unchanged: an attribute on its divf broke its own tests), and divides by y0 = 1 / Q (afn: the hardware's rcp), y = fma(fma(-Q, y0, 1), y0, y0), q0 = P y, q = fma(fma(-Q, q0, P), y, q0). erf 89 -> 76 instructions; new MLIR bench gelu_erf tput 36.9 -> 31.4 (-15%), 87 -> 74 instructions, cycles 236.1 -> 212.6; f16 / bf16 erf 13 instructions fewer; no other bench kernel has erf. Verified on agx_run against the general division: erf of all 2^32 f32 inputs, 0 results differ; f16 and bf16 erf over all 65,536 inputs, identical; without the correction (q = q0) 2,145,085 results in [0.5, 1) differ. lit gpu-to-air-erf-division.mlir. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E500 | **Adopted: E282's reciprocal step for 1 / (a + c) and 1 / fma(k, a, c), a never below 0** (AGXCodeGenPrepare reciprocalsAboveOne, `-agx-reciprocal-above-one-non-negative`; k > 0 and c >= 1 constants, a's class from computeKnownFPClass, either fma operand order): E282 took 1 / (e^x + c) only; any a that is never below 0 (|x|) makes d at least 1, +inf or a NaN as well, which E282's rcp, two fmas and +0 past 2^126 give as the division does. erfc (GPUToAIR's, 1 / (|x| + 2) and 1 / fma(|x|, 2, 1)) 97 -> 91 instructions; no agx-bench or MLIR bench kernel has the form (unchanged). Verified on agx_run against E498: erfc of all 2^32 f32 inputs, 0 results differ; 1 / (x + 2) of a possibly negative x is left alone, identical over [-1.5, -3]; a sabotage taking every a as never negative gives a NaN for 1 / (x + 2) at x = -2 instead of +inf. lit reciprocal-above-one.ll (abs_plus, abs_fma, plain_plus). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E501 | **Adopted: atan's one division by E282's reciprocal step and Markstein's correction** (AGXMathExpansion quotientAboveOne, `-agx-atan-quotient-step`): atanNonNegative divides -1 / a (a > tan(3 pi / 8)) or (a - 1) / (a + 1) (a in (tan(pi / 8), tan(3 pi / 8)]), its result used only there: the divisor is at least 1.41 (+inf, a NaN), the numerator 0, -1 or at least 2^-24 in magnitude (a - 1 exact near 1). So y = E282's reciprocal (rcp, two fmas, +0 past 2^126), q0 = n y, q = fma(fma(-d, q0, n), y, q0) (E409's argument), with q = q0 itself past 2^126 (an infinite d's correction would be inf * 0: a first build gave atan(+-inf) = NaN, found by the full sweep) in place of the general division's exponent scaling and special cases; a half or bfloat result's fast reciprocal product (E329) kept where it applied. f32 atan 59 -> 49 instructions (352 -> 284 bytes), atan2 550 -> 500 bytes, the half / bfloat atan + atan2 kernels 50 bytes smaller; no agx-bench or MLIR bench kernel has atan (unchanged). Verified on agx_run against E500: f32 atan of all 2^32 inputs, 0 results differ; f32 atan2 over 25,165,824 pairs (random words, wide-range normals, 256 pairs of specials), identical; f16 and bf16 atan of every input and atan2 of every x against 4 sets of y, identical; without the correction (q = q0) 618,094 results in [0.5, 1) differ. lit atan-quotient-step.ll; math-half-fast.ll's OFF run with the option off. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E502 | **Adopted: sincos's cosine selects its polynomial by the sine's odd-quadrant test, operands swapped** (AGXMathExpansion sinAndCos, `-agx-sincos-shared-odd`): cos is sin a quadrant ahead, so its select was by (q + 1) & 1 and its sign by (q + 1) & 2, while q + 1 is odd exactly where q is not: sel((q + 1) & 1, s, c) = sel(q & 1, c, s), one `and` of q's low bit for both halves of a sincos (the DAG did not see through the add). f32 sincos 71 -> 70 instructions (416 -> 410 bytes); a cos alone has the same count (its add moves next to its one use); sin alone, half sin / cos / sincos and bfloat sin / sincos unchanged. MLIR bench rope_f32 (math.sin and math.cos of one angle, merged into sincos) tput 28.4 -> 27.7, 75 -> 74 instructions, cycles 307.1 -> 306.0; nothing else in the MLIR bench or agx-bench changed. Verified on agx_run against E501: f32 sincos (both results) of all 2^32 inputs, 0 results differ; f32 cos alone over [0x3e000000, 0x41000000) and [0xc0000000, 0xc0c00000), identical; every bfloat cos identical. Sabotage (the swapped select's operands unswapped): 4,194,304 cosines in [0.5, 0.75) and 38,133 bfloat cosines differ. lit sincos-shared-odd.ll; fp.ll's autogenerated checks regenerated (the same instructions reordered). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E503 | **Adopted: tan as sin / cos's polynomials by quadrant without either half's sign** (AGXMathExpansion sinAndCos ForQuotient, `-agx-tan-quotient-signs`; f32 tan took div(sin(x), cos(x)) of the two intrinsics' expansions, half / bfloat tan sinAndCos's pair): sin's sign is bit 1 of q, cos's bit 1 of q + 1, which differ exactly where q is odd, and there sin / cos is (+-c) / (-+s): so tan = sel(odd, -c, s) / sel(odd, s, c), (-c) / s being -(c / s) exactly (the division, or a narrow result's reciprocal product, is symmetric in sign); past 2^22 the hardware's sin / cos as before. Bit 1 of q and of q + 1, the add and the two sign flips and selects go: f32 tan 88 -> 82 instructions (538 -> 492 bytes), half 68 -> 62, bfloat 72 -> 66; no agx-bench or MLIR bench kernel has tan. Verified on agx_run against E502: every half and bfloat tan identical; f32 tan of all 2^32 inputs, 0 results differ. Sabotage (the -c without its minus): 2,950 bfloat tangents and 3,600,420 f32 tangents in [0.5, 1) differ. lit tan-quotient-signs.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E504 | **Adopted: sin and cos's hardware fallback past 2^22 in a block that only SIMD-groups needing it run** (new AGXRareFallbacks pass after the SimplifyCFG before structurization, `-agx-rare-hardware-branch`, `-agx-rare-fallbacks-min` 2; AGXMathExpansion marks the fallback calls `!agx.rare`): sinAndCos computed the hardware's afn sin / cos (x / 2 pi, floor, sin_pt_1 / 2: 7-8 instructions each) for every thread and selected by |x| < 2^22. The selects of one block and condition whose false value is a marked call (used only there, its operands before the first select) become phis of a triangle: the calls move to a block branched to where the condition is false. At least 2 per block: one fallback alone is 8 instructions, which AGXLowerControlFlow runs with an empty mask rather than jump over (-agx-max-unskipped-instrs 12), so sin or cos alone are unchanged (selects of an expansion's unused half are not counted). In AGXCodeGenPrepare itself the pre-isel SimplifyCFG speculated the calls back into selects. sincos and tan (both its calls built before the range selects) qualify. MLIR bench rope_f32 (sincos) tput 27.7 -> 23.0 (-17%), cycles 306.0 -> 281.1, 15 -> 13 GPRs (76 static instructions, 2 more); standalone (55 groups): f32 sincos tput 23.6 -> 20.5, f32 tan 28.1 -> 23.9, bfloat tan 22.0 -> 17.4, half tan 20.8 -> 15.7 (one group's cycles up: the jump waits, e.g. tan 158.5 -> 165.2); nothing else in the MLIR bench or agx-bench changed. Verified on agx_run against E503: f32 sincos (both results) of all 2^32 inputs, 0 results differ; every half and bfloat tan identical; f32 sincos and tan of 8 x 2^22 random words and mixed small / large arguments (698,405 SIMD-groups with both kinds of thread) and of ranges across 2^22 and infinity identical. Sabotage (the phi of sincos's cosine without the hardware value): 1,736,529 cosines of the random words differ. lit rare-fallbacks.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E505 | **Adopted: what follows E504's fallback selects and needs neither them nor a write before it, ahead of the branch** (AGXRareFallbacks, `-agx-rare-fallbacks-hoist`): E504's split put the rest of the block after the join, so rope_f32's loads of x (after math.sin / math.cos in the source; one block's scheduler had started them first) waited until after the if. Now the instructions after the first select that use no select (or an instruction kept back), have no side effect, are not convergent and, if they read memory, follow no write move before the selects. rope_f32 tput 23.0 -> 22.7, cycles 281.1 -> 281.0 (13 GPRs, 76 instructions); nothing else in the MLIR bench or agx-bench changed. Verified on agx_run against E504: a rotation kernel (angle loaded, sincos, both x loads after it, two stores) over 6 x 2^20 threads of random words and wide-range normals, and one with a load of y[j + z] (z = 0 at run time) between the stores over 4 x 2^20, identical. Sabotage (loads moved past a write): 4,186,117 results of the second differ. lit rare-fallbacks-hoist.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E506 | **Adopted: E187's device-load loops unrolled 8 times where their loads step through two or more pointer bases and they store nothing** (AGX TTI deviceUnrollFactor, `-agx-device-unroll-streams` 8; opt): E187 kept 4 for f32 loops because 8 hurt them, but measured again (all MLIR bench loops at agx-device-unroll 8) it splits by the loads' streams: the dot products, two loads (row and vector) per element, gained (dot_i8 1745.3 -> 1636.8, dot_vec4 1428.0 -> 1268.7, gemv_q8f 1424.3 -> 1394.6, two_index 201.6 -> 196.6), the one-stream loops lost (rowsum 311.7 -> 382.2, online_softmax_vec4 11548.5 -> 11784.6, argmax_rows 342.8 -> 348.8, rowmax_vec4 282.6 -> 285.4). A stream is the SCEV pointer base of a device load whose address is an add recurrence of the loop. With the rule, exactly those four kernels change, each better (tput; GPRs 37 -> 46, 44 -> 76, 40 -> 58, 16 -> 25; gemv_q8f's one-group cycles 14881.1 -> 30142.9 though its tput improves); factor 16 was mixed (dot_vec4 1333.0, dot_i8 1592.8). agx-bench (no opt) unchanged. Verified on agx_run: the four kernels with and without the option on 3 seeds of data (wide-range floats with infinities, -0 and NaNs; random int8), outputs identical (unrolling keeps each accumulator's order); dot_i8 equals NumPy's integer product. lit device-loads-streams.ll; device-loads-narrow.ll's half-and-float loop (two bases) now 8 (16 with E507). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E507 | **Adopted: E506's two-stream loops unrolled 16 times where every device load is of 4 bytes at most** (AGX TTI deviceUnrollFactor, `-agx-device-unroll-streams-word` 16): E506's factor 16 trial lost only on dot_vec4, whose loads are 16-byte vectors (1268.7 -> 1333.0, 76 -> 117 GPRs), and gained on the others; with the rule only the kernels whose loads are scalars or vector<4xi8> change: two_index (scalar f32 rows) tput 196.6 -> 175.8, cycles 3233.2 -> 2232.4, 25 -> 41 GPRs (55 -> 40 groups); dot_i8 1636.8 -> 1592.8 (46 -> 50 GPRs); gemv_q8f (its x is vector<4xf32>) keeps 8; nothing else in the MLIR bench changed, agx-bench (no opt) unchanged. Verified on agx_run: dot_i8 and two_index with and without the option on 3 seeds, outputs identical. lit device-loads-streams.ll (a scalar dot product: 16, 8 with the option off), device-loads-narrow.ll's half-and-float loop 16. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E508 | **Fixed (tput): E506 / E507's factors only for loops whose device loads are all vectors** (AGX TTI deviceUnrollFactor, `-agx-device-unroll-streams-vectors`): the newk kernels (scratch MLIR set, not run for E506 / E507) found two regressions: gemv (a thread per row, scalar f32 row and vector) tput 244.1 -> 324.0 at 16 (356.3 at 8, though one group's cycles fell 6009.7 -> 5908.3) and rowdot (two scalar rows) 478.3 -> 649.5 (691.8 at 8). The scalar two-stream loop that gained (two_index) differs from them in no property the unroller sees. Now the rule asks every device load to be a vector (MLIR's vector.load: dot_vec4, dot_i8, gemv_q8f, newk dot_f16x4); scalar loops keep 4. Against E187's 4 on the MLIR bench and all 123 newk kernels, only improvements: dot_i8 1745.3 -> 1592.8, dot_vec4 1428.0 -> 1268.7, gemv_q8f 1424.3 -> 1394.6, dot_f16x4 856.4 -> 725.8; two_index back to 201.6 (E507's 175.8 withdrawn), gemv and rowdot unchanged. agx_run: the five kernels with and without the streams factor on 3 seeds, identical. lit device-loads-streams.ll (a scalar dot product 4, 16 with the option off; <4 x i8> 16), device-loads-narrow.ll's half-and-float loop 4 again. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E509 | **Adopted: E235's strided-load loops unrolled 16 times (8) and E234's narrow-load loops 32 (16)** (AGX TTI, `-agx-device-unroll-strided` 16, `-agx-device-unroll-narrow` 32): after E506-E508 the other factors swept on the MLIR bench and the 123 newk kernels (strided 4 / 16 / 32, narrow 32 / 64, store 8). Strided 16 changes only colsum (MLIR bench: tput 458.2 -> 372.5, cycles 12268.1 -> 7372.1, 14 -> 22 GPRs; newk's 238.4 -> 190.3); narrow 32 only rowsum_f16 (268.6 -> 223.6), newk3 dot_i8 (scalar bytes: 1249.8 -> 1182.7) and newk gemv_f16 (174.5 -> 168.5); together the same, nothing else changed, agx-bench (no opt) unchanged. Strided 32 (colsum 341.0, 31 GPRs, 48 groups) and narrow 64 (rowsum_f16 214.0, 40 GPRs) gain a little more on one kernel each at twice the registers: not taken. Strided 4 lost (attn_decode_f16 8864.8 -> 10396.7, colsum 549.5), store 8 lost (rowcopy_vec4 141.8 -> 155.7). Verified on agx_run: the five changed kernels with the old and new factors on 3 seeds (wide-range f32 with infinities, -0 and NaNs; halves; random int8), outputs identical. lit device-loads-narrow.ll (32), device-loads-store-strided.ll (16). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E510 | **Measured, no change: opt's general unroll threshold and E187's base factor on the MLIR bench and newk** (after E509). -unroll-threshold=600: only newk nested2d changes, tput 2355.7 -> 2318.1 but cycles 19069.7 -> 39911.4. -agx-device-unroll=8 (the factor of loops no other rule covers): six kernels lose (gemv 244.1 -> 356.3, rowdot 478.3 -> 691.8, rowsum 311.7 -> 382.2, online_softmax_vec4 11548.5 -> 11784.6, argmax_rows, rowmax_vec4, matmul_naive) and two_index gains (201.6 -> 196.6): 4 stays. | measured | -- |
| L15-E511 | **Adopted: lane-strided loops over a constant trip count, and loops of a running maximum kept as loops** (convert-gpu-to-air constantTripLoops, `constant-trip-loops`; AGX TTI, `-agx-device-unroll-keep-loop`). MLIR's row loops `scf.for %j = %lane to %c1024 step %c32` narrowed to a counter against min(1024 - lane, ...): no constant trip count in LLVM (the lane's range is not folded into it), so E187-E509's unrolling never applied and each iteration waited for its load (softmax_f16: one 2-byte load, a wait, an operation). Now a loop from a lower bound of known values [lo, hi] (indexRange: constants, gpu.lane_id ([0, 32) or its upper_bound), gpu.thread_id (upper_bound, known block size), additions and multiplications by constants) to a constant ub by a constant step, where ceil((ub - lb) / step) is the same T >= 2 for lo and hi, runs k from 0 to T, iv = lb + k step (nuw nsw: at most hi + (T - 1) step < ub), the same iterations in the same order. Loops whose iv is compared are left (a window's j <= row: window_rowmax 110.0 -> 429.6 and causal_rowmax 219.6 -> 373.8 otherwise, LLVM bounding their iterations by the compare). Exposed, LLVM's full unrolling also took the loops of a running maximum, whose E444 selects AGXCodeGenPrepare rewrites only in a loop (newk rowmax_store: the generic maximum's four instructions per element, tput 69.7 -> 79.2): a device-load loop of a loop-carried llvm.maximum / minimum with more than 16 trips now gets FullUnrollMaxCount = its factor, and a factor of at least the trip count is halved (softmax_f16's 32-trip half loop, narrow 32: whole it was 646.1); 16 trips or fewer still whole (newk logsoftmax, softmax_f16 of 4 lost as loops). The TTI rule alone changes no MLIR bench or newk kernel. Together, against E510 on the MLIR bench and newk, only improvements: rmsnorm_f16 tput 130.0 -> 92.8 (-29%), online_softmax 429.9 -> 396.6, online_softmax_exp2 493.8 -> 463.2, online_softmax_maxnum 424.2 -> 387.4, softmax_f16 628.0 -> 600.6, newk rowmax_store 69.7 -> 47.2 (GPRs up: 25 -> 42, 24 -> 55); agx-bench (no MLIR) unchanged. Verified on agx_run: the six kernels with and without both on 2 seeds (wide-range f32 with -inf, -0 and NaNs, +-0 rows; halves), outputs identical. Sabotage (T - 1 iterations): softmax_f16 35,491 and online_softmax 321 bytes differ. lit gpu-to-air-constant-trip.mlir (64 trips; 1000 elements, uneven, left; lane * 4 by 128: 8; a compared iv left), device-loads-minmax.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E512 | **Rejected (tput): E511 for loops whose smaller lower bounds run one iteration more** (convert-gpu-to-air prototype, `constant-trip-tail`: the loop over the larger bound's T, then the last iteration, cloned, under lb + T step < ub). The one kernel it changes, xent_rows (1000 scores by lanes: 32 or 31 trips), cycles 6719.1 -> 5656.2 but tput 343.8 -> 396.1, 130 -> 342 instructions: T = 31 takes no unroll factor and LLVM unrolled it whole. Reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E513 | **Adopted: E464's trivial sides as one u 0 + 1 for maximum and exp** (AGXCodeGenPrepare onlineSoftmaxExps, `-agx-online-softmax-shared-one`): e^(a - u) and e^(b - u) were b > a ? e : 1 + (a - u) and b > a ? 1 + (b - u) : e, two additions; with maximum (a NaN in, a NaN out) the trivial side x - u is u - u in every case (b > a: u = b; otherwise u = a, a tie's other zero, or a NaN), so 1 + (x - u) is fma(u, 0, 1): 1, or a NaN for an infinite or NaN u. One fma for both. Not for maxnum / maximumnum (a NaN a gives u = b: 1 + (a - u) a NaN, u 0 + 1 is 1), nor for exp2, where online_softmax_exp2 lost two resident groups (463.2 -> 494.2, 45 -> 48 GPRs). Also tried and dropped: the argument as -|b - a| (exact but for a tie of zeros' exponential 1 either way; an or of the sign bit and the clamp on |d|), which lost on every kernel (online_softmax_vec4 12267.9, online_softmax 461.2: the exponentials no longer wait for u, overlap and take registers), and both together (online_softmax_vec4 10753.5 but online_softmax 417.1, exp2 509.8). MLIR bench tput: online_softmax_vec4 11548.5 -> 10207.3 (-11.6%, 561 -> 529 instructions, 58 -> 50 GPRs, 28 -> 32 groups), online_softmax 396.6 -> 365.9 (-7.7%), attn_decode_f16 8864.8 -> 8676.8 (-2.1%, 77 -> 75 GPRs, 20 -> 22 groups); nothing else in the MLIR bench, newk or agx-bench changed. Verified on agx_run against E511: e^(a - u), e^(b - u) of all 324 pairs of 18 specials (+-0, +-inf, NaN, +-1, +-1e-38, +-1e-45 denormals, +-3e38, +-88.7, -104, 100, 0.5) and 2^21 random and wide-range pairs, identical; online_softmax_vec4 and online_softmax on E469 data with +inf added and on signed-zero rows, attn_decode_f16, identical. Sabotage (1 for the fma): 69 of the special pairs differ. lit online-softmax-exp.ll (and its option off), sched-loads-first.ll (75 / 51 GPRs). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E514 | **Adopted: E513 for maxnum / maximumnum of a running value that is never a NaN** (onlineSoftmaxExps, `-agx-online-softmax-shared-one-num`): E513 left maxnum out because a NaN a gives u = b, where 1 + (a - u) is a NaN and u 0 + 1 is 1. An online softmax's a is the running maximum: a phi from a number (-inf) whose updates are maxnum with it, which keep a number; then u is b where b > a and a otherwise, and both trivial sides are u - u again. NeverNaN: a non-NaN constant; a maxnum / maximumnum with a never-NaN operand; a phi all of whose incoming values are, itself assumed one while its cycle is followed (an unrolled loop's chain, induction over the iterations). MLIR bench online_softmax_maxnum tput 387.4 -> 360.4 (-7.0%), 509 -> 477 instructions, 42 -> 40 GPRs; nothing else in the MLIR bench or newk changed (newk online_softmax_exp2_maxnum is exp2). Verified on agx_run against E513: online_softmax_maxnum on E469 data with +inf, on signed-zero rows and on rows of NaNs (all, every third, the first, NaN / +inf alternating) and +-inf rows, identical; online_softmax_vec4 and online_softmax identical. Sabotage (every a taken as never NaN): a maxnum pair kernel of loaded operands, which the rule leaves (identical), differs on 15 of the 324 special pairs. lit online-softmax-exp.ll (running_maxnum; pair_maxnum still adds). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E515 | **Measured, no change: what runtime alias checks (loop versioning on non-overlapping kernel arguments) would buy** (by hand: newk rowcopy_f16's two pointer arguments marked noalias in its optimized IR). Each iteration of a load / store loop over two arguments loads, waits and stores before the next load, as a store may alias the next load. With noalias the loads go ahead: cycles 11743.5 -> 2610.4, but 14 -> 39 GPRs (55 -> 42 resident groups) and tput 274.0 -> 310.0: with enough groups the waits are already hidden, so versioning loops on their arguments' ranges was not built. | measured | -- |
| L15-E516 | **Measured, no change: E513's fma placed after the maximum, exp2 again, and online_softmax_vec4 without loads first** (prototypes). Now the preheader (and a dedicated exit, LoopSimplify's) branches to the exit where the first iteration is masked: then every iteration is (the mask is monotone), each leaving every reduction as it was (E476's identities), so the result is their start values, which the exit's LCSSA phis take on the new edge. In the loop no iteration is masked: the tail compare is false (its inverse true), its selects take the unmasked value. After E481's raised start (the first iteration's mask is the final start's; done before it, a raised start past the tail's edge would have run masked with the mask folded). | L15-E527 | **Adopted: innermost loops that store to one device buffer and load from others versioned on their access ranges not overlapping** (new AGXAliasVersioning, before alignment versioning in llc's IR passes, `-agx-alias-versioning`; LoopAccessAnalysis's runtime checks and LoopVersioning, the copy's accesses in disjoint alias scopes). Candidates: single-block, simple device loads and stores only, no convergent operation (the per-thread check is divergent), at most 6 checks, no 1- or 2-byte elements (alignment versioning copies those loops again: four copies cost more fetching than they gain, newk rowcopy_f16 without its known grid +6.7% tput), at most 48 words loaded an iteration (`-agx-alias-versioning-max-words`: rowcopy_vec4's 64, all hoisted, are reverted by E239's thread rule anyway, leaving only the copy's cost, +11%) and at most 4 arithmetic operations per word (`-agx-alias-versioning-math-per-word`, a transcendental 16, a division 8: exp and sigmoid rows hide their loads behind the work and lost occupancy, +11% / +5.8%). **Harness:** agx_cost ran every kernel with its pointers 0, so all buffers overlapped and the check took the original loop; agx-bench's new pointer_uniforms (used by bench.py's cost too) gives each device buffer argument (and the front end's by-reference i64 that becomes a pointer) a high word of its own, in the cost and throughput runs, keeping uniforms a kernel's header sets: with E526's compiler agx-bench's 100 entries and all 187 MLIR bench / newk kernels cost the same either way. New MLIR bench layernorm_thread (a thread per 256-float row, three passes, checked against NumPy): tput 1159.5 -> 951.6 (-17.9%), cycles 45187.2 -> 22572.5, 24 -> 40 GPRs; newk20 (new: row loops a thread per row) row_norm_apply 1379.7 -> 1029.2 (-25.4%), row_relu 856.1 -> 648.9 (-24.2%), row_scale -3.5%, row_residual -2.5%, row_axpy -1.9%; row_exp, row_sigmoid, row_bias_gelu left (the arithmetic rule; gelu would have gained 4.5%); nothing else in the MLIR bench or newk changes (softmax_f16's store loop, E515's best case under noalias, is unrolled into straight-line code after a subgroup reduction: not a loop), nor agx-bench. agx_run: the old and new llc identical on the 8 row kernels and layernorm, full grids with separate buffers and single threads with y over x, w or z at 0, +-4, +-1024 and 2044 bytes (305 cases); with threads that overwrite inputs other threads read without a barrier (a data race) results can differ, as for any reordering. agx-stress 42 ok, fuzz 0 failing. (A scheduler rule that also reverted regions with device stores to their original order where they cut the core's groups 1.8 times was tried and changed nothing: removed.) Tests: alias-versioning.ll; narrow-signed-induction.ll pinned to -agx-alias-versioning=0. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E528 | **Adopted: E527 for straight-line runs, in opt after the last unrolling, kept where the copy then folds its reloads** (AGXAliasVersioningPass at the optimizer's last extension point, `-agx-alias-versioning-straight-line`). softmax_f16's third pass over its row (a SIMD-group a row; E515's best case under noalias, -29%) is unrolled into one block after its subgroup sum: each element stores y, then reloads x and recomputes the second pass's exp(x - m), since the store may have written x. A run is a block's stretch outside loops from a device store to the last access before a call or other memory operation (loads before its first store are left: no store of it holds them); its accesses are grouped as Base + Scale ext(Root) + constant (GEP indices split by SCEV into a root and a constant the 32-bit add cannot wrap with: the thread positions' and the kernel's bounds attached for the analysis only, as L14-E1 requires, so an unrolled zext(i + 32) is zext(i) + 32), and the thread's ranges of the groups checked as E527's. The copy (disjoint alias scopes) and EarlyCSE with MemorySSA (run only on a function so changed): softmax_f16's copy keeps per element an fmul, the truncation and the store, its loads and exps the second pass's values (copy cost 128 vs the run's 717, an instruction 1, a transcendental 16). A copy that does not halve the run's cost is undone, back to one block (E522's straight-line alignment versioning needs one: rmsnorm_f16 +25.7% while the split stayed): the trial's other runs (layernorm_rows, rmsnorm_f16, newk logsoftmax / rmsnorm / softmax_f16 of 1024, four to eight stores) folded nothing and as plain copies lost 14-100% tput (the llc scheduler's hoisting, at their registers). softmax_f16 tput 600.6 -> 402.4 (-33.0%), cycles 7817.2 -> 4295.6, 55 -> 89 GPRs; nothing else in the MLIR bench, newk or newk20 changes; agx-bench (llc only) unchanged. (The same runs versioned in llc, without the CSE, changed only rmsnorm_f16: +44.9%.) agx_run: softmax_f16 by the old and new opt / llc identical with separate buffers, in place and y at +-2, +-64, +-2048, +4096 bytes from x; agx-stress 42 ok, fuzz 0 failing. (At 8: matmul 138.3, colsum_dyn 323.1, but spmv_csr 45.0: 4 kept.) agx_run: matmul identical for K = 64, 63, 61, 6, 1 and 0 (every remainder); agx-stress 42 ok, fuzz 0 failing. These conditions separate the gains from the losses: row_cumsum tput 320.0 -> 284.0 (-11.2%), row_residual -7.5%, row_relu -5.0%, row_scale -4.2%, row_axpy -2.3%, reverse_rows -0.4% (their 16 iterations' loads held 32 registers: 48 -> 32 GPRs, 34 -> 52 groups; the cycles of one group rise, ~7400 -> ~14000). Excluded on the way, each lost at 8: byte stores (quant_rows +20.6%, to_u8 +22.9%), three loads (row_norm_apply +29.2%, layernorm +19.1%), transcendental or shared-unit work (popcount_rows +15.2%, bswap_rows +2.0%, row_exp +1.9%, row_bias_gelu +1.7%), 128 trips (gather_rows +1.5%), a column (col_softmax_norm +0.4%). | L15-E594 | **AGXWordIndices: a loop's word accesses at a 32-bit counter of bytes or halves indexed by a counter of words beside it** (`-agx-loop-word-indices`, a pre-ISel pass after AGXNarrowInductions): base + s * zext(iv + k) (+ an offset), the access 4-byte words (4 to 16 bytes of 4-byte elements, 2 or 4 halves at a word index, 8 halves where E585 / E591 make them one access, not what instruction selection indexes in its units already: 8 bytes at a half index (Via16), 4 bytes at a byte index), took a 64-bit add for each address (iadd64, 3.92 units of the core half's shared unit); now w = {start * s / 4, +, step * s / 4} and base' + 4 * zext(w + d), base' = base + the smallest offset (before the loop), one 32-bit add for each further offset. Only where scalar evolution proves iv and iv + k do not wrap (with the thread positions' bounds for the analysis, as E553: a runtime row's start 512 * r and LSR's counter beside a nsw exit counter of the same step), the start a known multiple, and no more further offsets than bases (nested2d's sixteen loads from one base doubled its cycles with fifteen adds between them). Found by hand-editing hsum_dyn's loop (309.6 -> 234.9). Corpus: row_axpy tput 705.2 -> 529.9 (-24.9%), row_relu 449.5 -> 349.3 (-22.3%), hsum_dyn / sum_f16_dyn 309.6 -> 248.0 (-19.9%), row_norm_apply 1082.7 -> 941.8 (-13.0%), reverse_rows -10.7%, inplace -9.8%, layernorm 956.5 -> 906.7 (-5.2%), row_residual -3.0%, row_bias_gelu, to_f16, row_scale -0.5% to -0.1%; worse: where_rows 354.5 -> 382.3 (+7.8%) and row_cumsum 239.1 -> 243.7 (+1.9%), both faster with fewer groups (where_rows 2 to 16 groups -16%, row_cumsum -11.6% at 40) and slower only at the cap, the saturated simulation's crossing; agx-bench identical. | L15-E613 | **log, log2 and log10's polynomial of fabs(x)** (`-agx-log-abs-core`), the one pow takes: every result of a negative x or a NaN is replaced by logSpecial, so the value is the same for every input, and in one block SelectionDAG makes log(x) and pow(x, y) one exponent split and one polynomial. | L15-E614 | **log's exponent e as the float 2^23 + e less 2^23 + 127** (`-agx-log-exponent-by-add`): the biased field ((bits >> 23) & 0xff) into the bits 2^23 leaves clear is one bfeil with 2^23 as its base (a new pattern for (x >> s) & m | c with c & m = 0), and one add instead of a 16-bit subtract and a convert. log_dyn tput 7338.1 -> 7044.0 (-4.0%), entropy_dyn 7349.7 -> 7046.1 (-4.1%), logpow_rows 6355.4 -> 6247.6 (-1.7%), acosh_f16 22.3 -> 21.6, asinh_f32 34.9 -> 34.7, logsoftmax 73.7 -> 73.1; a log once per row after the loops, one instruction fewer, moves within 1% either way (xent_rows 287.8 -> 290.6, online_softmax_maxnum 282.6 -> 284.1, online_softmax 287.1 -> 285.8). Bit-identical agx_run output against E612 for log, pow, log2 and log10 in one kernel and log alone over 8 x 2^20 random bit patterns. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E615 | **log's 2^e (1 + f) from x's doubled bits plus those of 1 less those of the float above sqrt(1/2)** (`-agx-log-offset-split`): the exponent field (from bit 24) is one more from a mantissa above sqrt(2)'s on, and the mantissa (from bit 1) plus 0x3f3504f4 is 1 + f in (sqrt(1/2), sqrt(2)], the e and 1 + f that the compare and two selects (and a halving) gave, for every positive finite x. Doubling is iadd's operand shift and drops the sign, so the and of |x|'s sign bit folds away and log of x and pow of |x| still share it; a bfeil pattern for (x >> s) | c with c clear in x >> s's bits takes the exponent field into 2^23's. Over E614: log_dyn tput 7044.0 -> 6650.5 (-5.6%), entropy_dyn 7046.1 -> 6806.1 (-3.4%), logpow_rows 6247.6 -> 6001.0 (-3.9%), asinh_f32 34.7 -> 33.8, acosh_f16 21.6 -> 21.1, logsoftmax 73.1 -> 71.9, xent_rows 290.6 -> 287.2; online_softmax_exp2 / _vec4 +0.2..0.3% (two instructions fewer in their one log per row). Nothing else changed. log-abs-core.ll and log-exponent.ll updated (the E613 field checks under `-agx-log-offset-split=0`), fp.ll regenerated. Bit-identical agx_run output against E612 over the 8 x 2^20 random bit patterns. | adopted | -- |

A runtime row length's loop has no such counter (row_relu_dyn: x and y at row * n + j, w at j). word-indices.ll gains exit and checks hsum's counter of halves gone. Bit-identical agx_run output for all fifteen constant-row kernels, at the full grid and in 43 one-thread runs each with every pointer into one buffer at offsets around the others, and for the eight runtime-row ones at row lengths 0, 1, 15, 16, 17, 37, 100, 255, 256, 511 and 512. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E624 | **A count of iterations from 0 that only the exit compare with a loop-invariant bound reads, as E594's counter of words against that bound in words** (AGXWordIndices, `-agx-loop-word-indices-exit-count`): an unrolled loop of a runtime row counts its elements from 0 by a power of two s_p up to n, a multiple of s_p below 2^31 by its known bits; its n / s_p-th increment is the one equal to n, and the counter of words is then w.start + n / s_p * s_w, which no earlier one equals mod 2^32 where n's bound / s_p * s_w < 2^32: that bound computed before the loop, the count goes, and a phi after the loop of the count takes n. hsum_dyn / sum_f16_dyn tput 237.2 -> 228.6 (-3.6%), rms_f16_dyn 965.4 -> 956.8 (-0.9%), bsum_dyn 438.9 -> 437.8 (-0.3%), softmax_f16_dyn 6549.5 -> 6554.0 (+0.1%), four instructions more each before the loop; nothing else changed. word-indices.ll gains count. Bit-identical agx_run output for the five at row lengths 0 to 512 as in E623. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E625 | **E624 against any other counter of the loop by a positive power of two, not only a counter of words** (`-agx-loop-exit-on-step-counter`), and for a 64-bit bound below 2^32 by its known bits (truncated before the loop: an unrolled loop's n & 0x7ffffff8): that counter's add loses its no-wrap flags, as it is now compared on the last iteration too. Over E624, 27 runtime-row kernels changed: cumsum_dyn tput 462.5 -> 344.2 (-25.6%), split_dyn 940.4 -> 886.8 (-5.7%), iadd_i32_dyn 361.0 -> 341.4 (-5.4%), deq_i8_f16_dyn -4.1%, row_axpy_dyn -4.0%, hscale_dyn -3.7%, q_f16_i8_dyn -3.4%, rowmix_dyn -3.4%, lrelu_f16_dyn -2.4%, gelu_dyn -1.3%, and nine more by 0.4-0.9%, clamp_dyn level, but quant_dyn 414.1 -> 421.9 (+1.9%, 32 -> 30 GPRs: the same loop with its second wait earlier), softmax_f16_dyn +0.6%, hdot_dyn / softmax_dyn +0.4%, layernorm_dyn +0.3%, rms_f16_dyn +0.2%, row_scale_dyn +0.0%; agx-bench unchanged. word-indices.ll gains step. Bit-identical agx_run output for the 27 at row lengths 0 to 512 as in E623. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E626 | **E624 / E625 where the count is also read after the loop, beyond its exit block** (`-agx-loop-exit-count-uses-after`): an unrolled loop's remainder starts at the count, read in a block after the exit block; where only the latch enters that exit block, the latch is the loop's only exiting block and the exit block dominates the use, every path there leaves the loop with the count equal to the bound, which the use takes. Bit-identical agx_run output for the thirteen at row lengths 0 to 512 as in E623. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E627 | **Rejected: loop alias versioning of column loops through no-wrap predicates** (prototype `-agx-alias-versioning-max-predicates`, with `-agx-alias-versioning-small-step` raised to 65536 so that E565's retry with the thread positions' ranges reaches a column's 32 KiB step). The runtime-row column loops (col_axpy_dyn, col_relu_dyn, col_scale_dyn: one element a row, the row stride n floats) have no versioned copy: LAA wants 100 checks for the unrolled loop (each access a group of its own) and, with the ranges, 2 checks and predicates that the indices do not wrap, which the pass refuses. A 16-bit destination's order puts the other halves of its sources' registers last, and a source (16-bit, or a 32-bit value read by halves) the register of an assigned destination's other half; among the registers already in use first, so that no register is opened for it (opening them took tiny kernels from 2 to 3 and 3 to 4 GPRs). | L15-E636 | **A versioned loop's range checks as compares of the starts' differences** (AGXAliasVersioning diffChecks, `-agx-alias-versioning-diff-checks`, `-agx-alias-versioning-diff-runtime`; the straight-line runs of E528 too): LoopVersioning compared four ends a pair, a < b + n and b < a + m, each 64-bit, of starts that held the thread's own row offset (r * 128 in a and b alike). The ranges [a, a + m) and [b, b + n) overlap where -n < b - a < m, that is b - a + n - 1 <u m + n - 1: one compare, and b - a is the buffers' difference alone, the same for every thread (exact for addresses that do not wrap; m = n = 0 a conflict, the loop as it was). | L15-E638 | **c - 2 y and the like as one fma where the scaling is exact** (AGXMathExpansion, `-agx-math-fused-exact-scales`): tanh's 1 - 2 / (e^2a + 1) took 2 y (an add of y to itself) and 1 - 2 y; y is the reciprocal of d in [2, e^18 + 1], at least 1.5e-8, so 2 y is exact and fma(-2, y, 1) rounds 1 - 2 y once, as the subtraction did. Also asin's pi / 2 - 2 p, asin's and acos's (1 - a) / 2 as 0.5 - 0.5 a (1 - a is exact where the square root's result is read, a in [0.5, 1]; any a > 1 gives a negative argument either way, the square root's NaN) and halfTurns' x - 2 round(x / 2) (sinpi, cospi, tanpi). | L15-E658 | **Double division's significand quotient in two 28-bit digits** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-div-digits`): agx_cost charges the 64-bit adds, the 32 x 32 -> 64-bit multiply-adds and the bit-field, shift and conversion class to one unit per half-core (2 to 4 cycles each), and the reciprocal to 2^-60 from 64-bit products (14 wide multiply-adds, 22 64-bit adds: 206 of its cycles) made fdiv the costliest double operation (tput 255.4, fadd 44.4). Now one Newton step from the agx_rcp estimate Y0 (below 2^82 / Dv by less than 2^-18 of it) to a 32-bit Y1 (below 2^83 / Dv by less than 1.04, the residual's high word the complement of Dv Y0's), then q1 = (N >> 22) Y1 >> 35 and q2 = (R1 >> 22) Y1 >> 33, each floor or one less (errors at most 0.31 and 0.79 units), with the exact remainders R1 = N 2^26 - q1 Dv and R2 = R1 2^28 - q2 Dv (below 2 Dv, modulo 2^64) and one correction; 6 wide multiply-adds, 6 64-bit adds. fdiv tput 255.4 -> 147.5 (-42%), expm1_f64 -7.0%, sinh_f64 -7.3%, tanh_f64 -7.3%, cosh_f64 -7.1%. A Python model over 1.2M divisors and dividends (2^52 + 0..63, 2^53 - 1 - 0..63, random; N near Dv and 2 Dv) with Y0 errors up to 2^-18.4: every quotient and remainder exact. agx_run against IEEE (numpy): 168M divisions (normal, mid-range, edge significands, denormals) and all 2^23 values of agx_rcp's input (the divisor's top 23 fraction bits) twice, bit-identical. soft-f64-div-digits.ll added. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E659 | **Double fma with a constant addend: a short path for a product and the addend two or more places apart** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-fma-far`, `-agx-soft-f64-fma-far-any-addend` to test it): the product P = MX MY 2 (top bit at 105 or 106, bit 0 zero) and Z at its place u in P's units; where P is the bigger (u <= 51) Z goes into P's 128 bits with the bits it loses ORed into its lowest (a round to odd at P's zero bit 0), where Z is the bigger (u >= 56) P's bits from 44 up with a sticky bit go under Z's significand 10 places up, also with a sticky bit; either sum has its top bit at 60 to 63, a normalization of at most three places instead of a 128-bit count of leading zeros and shift. Closer operands take E's earlier path in a conditional block of its own, then the general code. For every fma it made cuda_ddot 48% slower (its accumulator s = fma(x[i], y[i], s) is often as large as the product, and a SIMD group with such a lane ran both paths: 7382 dynamic instructions against 4668), so only for a constant addend (a polynomial's coefficient, which a product rarely comes close to: all of the f64 kernels' fmas). fma with constant y and z tput 99.7 -> 78.5 with it alone. soft-f64-fast.ll's fma_coefficient added. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E660 | **A 64-bit add or subtract of a value with a zero low word: a 32-bit add of the high words** (AGXISelLowering `-agx-add-high-words`, AGXISD::ADD_HI / SUB_HI): the soft-double rounding's exponent, (E - 1) << 52 added to the significand, was a 64-bit iadd (4 cycles on the shared unit) for a carry-free add of the high word; known bits of the other operand's low 32 bits all zero, the low word is kept and the high words added (an ALU iadd). Two 64-bit adds per short path (fdiv 108 -> 100 shared-unit cycles); also saxpy_dyn -12%, sdivs_dyn -20%, idiv_const -17% (their 64-bit index arithmetic), red16 and reduce_f16_i64 +1.2% (one iadd instead of an iadd64 and a move: layout). add-high-words.ll added. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E661 | **Double significand products as four 32 x 32 -> 64-bit multiply-adds with the partial sums as addends** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-mul-limbs`; AGXInstructions patterns for a zero-extended 32-bit addend): the 128-bit multiplication's expansion added its partial products with 64-bit iadds after multiply-adds of a zero addend (an addend zext i32 or a pair with a zero high word lost to IADD_I64_I32's pattern). Now limbs below 2^22 keep every partial sum below 2^64 and the two carries into the top product a 32-bit add; the patterns give the multiply-add the addend (a pair with a mov_imm 0). fmul's short path 64 -> 44 shared-unit cycles, fma 158 -> 132; fmul tput 45.1 -> 32.7 together with E660. float-misc.ll, soft-f64.ll and soft-f64-fast.ll check mul i64 for mul i128; add-high-words.ll's mad_zext added. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E662 | **Double short paths: a normal result told from its unrounded exponent, and an addition's operand classes from the exponents it extracts** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-range-from-exponent`): an E of 1 to 2045 is a normal result after any rounding carry, without extracting the result's field (the top binade, E 2046, takes the general path); addFast checked X and Y again after selecting Big and Small, whose exponent fields it has. fadd's short path 60 -> 54 shared-unit cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E663 | **Double significands added or subtracted with one addition** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-one-add`): Same ? A + X : A - X as M ^ ((A ^ M) + X) with M the sign mask (A - X = ~(~A + X)), not both and a select: one 64-bit add fewer in fadd, two to three in fma's 128-bit and 64-bit sums. fma's short path 130 -> 118 shared-unit cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E664 | **Double multiplication rounded from the product's words** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-mul-direct`): the significand is the product's bits from 52 + top up (top: whether it has 106 bits, its top word at or above 2^9), two funnel shifts (extr) of words 1 to 3, the guard bit below and a sticky bit for the rest, instead of the top 64 bits, a conditional shift and a shift by 11; and the exponent kept as its field in the high word: (E - 1) << 20 is X's and Y's fields 20 places up (masks, below 2^32 together) less 1024 << 20 plus top << 20, and a normal result's is below 2045 << 20 modulo 2^32 (E - 1 is -1023 to 3069), with no field extracted. fmul's short path 42 -> 32 shared-unit cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E665 | **Double division rounded from the quotient's digits** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-div-direct`): Q = q1 2^28 + low with q1 2^28 a multiple of 4, so the significand Q >> 2 is (q1 << 26) + (low >> 2), the guard bit low's bit 1 and the sticky bit its bit 0 or the remainder, instead of Q << 1 with the sticky bit, 8 places up and 11 down; the exponent in its field as in E664 ((E - 1) << 20 the fields' difference plus (1022 - less) << 20). fdiv's short path 98 -> 86 shared-unit cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E666 | **Double addition with the significands 3 places up, not 10** (AGXSoftF64 `-agx-soft-f64-add-frame3`): one funnel shift for the high word and an iadd shift for the low one, the implicit bit ORed for both (a zero Small shifted 64 places, only a sticky bit, which rounds back to Big; two zeros selected apart), the exponent in its field. Three zero bits are enough: a Small shifted by at most one place loses nothing, and shifted by two or more the sum's top bit is within a place of Big's, its sticky bit then at most 9 places up, below the guard bit. fadd's short path 52 -> 42 shared-unit cycles. A first version that left a zero Small's shift at Big's exponent difference added 2^(55 - D) for it and failed 0.04% of agx_run's sums; the op tester's close mode (y within 40 ulps of +-x, 0 to 3 places apart) added. soft-f64-short-rounding.ll (E664 - E666) added. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E667 | **E658 - E666 together, against E648** (the committed compiler): double operation tput (one operation per thread, loads and a store) fadd 44.4 -> 35.5 (-20%), fmul 45.1 -> 27.0 (-40%), fma 111.8 -> 94.8 (-15%), fma with constant y and z 99.7 -> 58.3 (-42%), fdiv 255.4 -> 137.7 (-46%), fmul by a constant 40.1 -> 22.5 (-44%). The ten corpus kernels and cuda_ddot give bit-identical agx_run output; cuda_permute4d's and cuda_attn_decode's code differs only by E661's multiply-add with the paired addend. agx_run against IEEE (numpy, math.fma with exact overflow), 262144 operations a run, 30 seeds: fma in five operand modes (random, mid-range, near cancellation, the product and addend 120 places either side of each other, overflow and underflow ranges) by default and with `-agx-soft-f64-fma-far-any-addend`, fmul, fadd, fsub and fdiv in four (with close operands), all bit-identical (1260 runs); 56 more division runs including all 2^23 reciprocal inputs twice. llc time on the largest double kernels +12% (tanh_f64) and +21% (expm1_f64), 0.05 to 0.08 s. | measured | -- |

The loop whose own blocks hold such products (an atomic's compare-exchange loop may be inside it) runs as a copy where every such y scaled fits in 32 bits, each test (y - lo) >u (hi - lo) in the preheader, whose products are of sext(shl nsw i32 y, k) (a signed multiply-add with the base as its addend), and as it was otherwise; only the products' operands change (a pointer's step keeps the 64-bit value: replacing every use in the loop made strength reduction step the source pointer with one more 64-bit add, cycles +6.2%); alias versioning's fallbacks are left; with convergent operations only for uniform y (from arguments, constants, the threadgroup's size). cuda_index_add tput 418.6 -> 296.0 (-29.3%, cycles 7889.0 -> 7686.8) on agx-g14c, 533.3 -> 465.7 (-12.7%) on agx-g13g (its float atomic a compare-exchange loop inside the versioned one); nothing else in agx-bench or the MLIR corpus changed. agx_run of a loop storing (long)x * (y * 4) + (long)x * (y * 12) for 18 values of y at and across both limits (2^29 - 1, -2^29, 2^29, 178956970, 178956971, the i32 extremes) and 9472 x each: bit-identical to Python's integers; cuda_index_add's scatter on agx_run (64 rows of 256 integer-valued floats) the reference sums with either compiler. wide-index-versioning.ll added. | adopted | -- |

| L15-E674 | **A loop's 64-bit byte counters of word accesses as one 32-bit counter of words, versioned on its range** (AGXWordCounterVersioning, `-agx-loop-word-counters`, `-agx-loop-word-counters-max-bases` 2, in addPreISel after AGXNarrowInductions): the PTX front end's byte offsets stay 64-bit counters after strength reduction (cuda_attn_decode's K and V addresses, p += d for a runtime d, two 64-bit adds per iteration on the shared unit); E671's byte offset was no index, a counter of words is (a device access's index register counts its format's elements). Header phis stepped by the same loop-invariant runtime d whose other uses are inttoptr (or base + counter, constant offsets) to device loads and stores of whole words at least word aligned: a copy where d is a multiple of 4 and the count of words over the loop fits in 32 bits, with one i32 counter of words from 0 (one 32-bit add) and each access gep i32 (its counter's start), w; the loop as it was otherwise. The range from scalar evolution's symbolic maximum count, or, without one, from the latch's c + s < n (slt or ult, s and n invariant): s > 0, n + s - 1 in the counter's range (no wrap), and (n - c0) abs(d / 4) < 2^31 s, no division; with convergent operations (attn_decode's shuffles) the test is a SIMD-group ballot, one copy for the group. The same values: a NaN residual makes the result a NaN, and a finite residual's result is finite there (silu: q0, y and r' finite for finite x; 1 / sqrt: y0 finite and nonzero wherever e is finite), and at x = +-inf or a NaN both take q0. InstCombine leaves the select (p + -0 is not p for an arbitrary p: a NaN's payload, a flushed denormal). The select of v and a constant reads v (its compare reads v and the threshold), a 1-cycle compare-select where select(c, p + v, p) read the compare's operand, p + v and p (2 cycles). Each gep is now collected once. Nothing in the MLIR corpus or agx-bench changes. The same sweep (MLIR corpus tput): offset-index-max 2 shflall -13.5%, conv3x3 -7.9%, silu_f16x4 -2.8% but one group's cycles +22% to +31% (E392's trade, left); addressing-modes 2 and upper-bound-ranges 0 / 2 mixed (hscale_dyn +230% at 0), cold-regions-percent 0 lost (sinh_f64 +23%), the others nothing. lit 546 (rebase-loop-indices-shared.ll, crashed before), agx-stress 42 ok, fuzz 0 failing. | adopted | -- |

Each seed: every thread updates its row of 256 words in one to three statements b[x + i + o] = f(b[x + i + o'] or a constant, c[...]) in program order (an earlier statement's or iteration's store read back), c an input buffer at i + o'' or x + i + o'', or b itself passed twice (alias versioning's overlapping copy); a constant or input trip, guarded or not, steps of 1 or 2; f an exact 32-bit op (add, sub, xor, or, and, a constant multiply or shift, umax); llc, and every other seed opt -O2 first. The E768 compiler fails 4 of 2000 seeds (E769's crash), the E769 one none; agx-stress 44 ok. | adopted | -- |

The result is computed on each edge into a phi after the loop; the edge was split with SplitEdge, which for an edge that is not critical (the latch into a dedicated exit, its one predecessor) splits the exit block after its phis and returns the second half, so the phi kept the latch's incoming value, the chain's last link, which the rewrite then erased: poison, a wrong maximum. Seen first with agx-mlir-fuzz.py's new reductions (a running arith.maximumf carried by scf.for's iter_args and stored after a guarded loop, a runtime trip: 23 of 2000 seeds wrong, every one f32 maximum, the stored values even below the initial value), and gone with -agx-deferred-minmax-reductions=0. And accurate where the old form was not: 1 + tanh(t) for t below about -1 is 1 less a tanh within an ulp of -1, the error the whole result (1 + tanh(-26) gave 5.96e-8 for 4.4e-23, GELU(-90.6) -2.7e-6 for -0). Against double's stable 2 / (1 + e^-2t) over 16.8M inputs (8.4M random words, 8.4M over [-60, 60], specials): results at or above 2^-125 at most 2.44 ulps (122 above 2, 220897 above 1), the old form up to 2.1e37 ulps (2982060 above 4); below 2^-125 (t in (-44.0, -43.7)) the reciprocal flushes to 0 a result the old form also gave as 0; NaN, +-inf and -0 as before; 1 - tanh symmetric. agx-mlir-fuzz.py's math rows gain 1 + tanh and GELU (the stable double reference): the E773 compiler fails 9 of 1000 seeds there, this one none of 4000. lit 548 (tanh-plus-one.ll), agx-stress 46 ok, fuzz 0 failing. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E789 | **E504's rare-fallback branch for the fallbacks of every condition in a block, one branch where any of them is false** (AGXRareFallbacks, `-agx-rare-fallbacks-merge`): an unrolled loop's sins each have their own condition (abs(x) < 2^22) and one hardware fallback, too few for E504's branch, so every thread computed every fallback (x / 2 pi, floor, sin_pt_1 / 2: 7 instructions each). Now a block's groups share one branch: what follows the first select and needs none of them is hoisted ahead of it (E505's rule), the selects whose operands are then ready are taken, the condition is whether any of theirs is false (a chain of compare-selects, no i1 values and'ed), and in the rare block each value is the select of its own condition again (its comparison cloned there, one fcmpsel). Repeated until no group is left (a select behind a store is in the first branch's tail); a moved fallback loses its mark (else the rare block's selects were taken again, endlessly). sin_dyn (4 a loop iteration) and sincos_rows (sin x, cos 2x). agx_cost's geometry paths take every branch on loaded data, so MLIR corpus tput: sincos_rows 4803.6 -> 3662.1 (-23.8%, 31 -> 38 GPRs), but sin_dyn 5041.8 -> 5591.9 (+10.9%: the branch, never skipped there, is pure cost); nothing else in the corpus or agx-bench changed. With the functional path over mapped inputs: abs(x) < 10 sin_dyn 277298.6 -> 211762.1 cycles (-23.6%), sincos_rows 4803.6 -> 3662.1 a group; every argument past 2^22 +10.9% and +14.1%; 1% of them past 2^22 (most SIMD-groups take the branch) -1.8% and +3.2%. Adopted on the functional numbers: arguments past 2^22 are the rare case the fallback exists for. Bit-identical agx_run output against the merged compiler on random words and wide-range normals (two seeds, the corpus's sin / cos / tan kernels); a sabotaged rare select (operands swapped) differs. rare-fallbacks.ll four_sins, heavy-stores-apart.ll without the merge. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E790 | **(Merged from main, recorded there as E220.) Adopted: SIMD-group aggregation of atomic additions, divergent addresses included** (AGXExpandWideAtomics expandAggregate, `-agx-aggregate-atomics=32` rounds, `-agx-aggregate-atomics-min=2`): native 32-bit add / fadd (fadd not with ieee-fp32), device or generic, monotonic: the threads on the lowest pending thread's address add their simd_reduce sum with one atomic (results: the leader's old value shuffled plus simd_prefix; the lowest thread keeps the old bits exactly), repeated while a group has >= 2 threads; a uniform address takes one round without the address test. Hardware (2026-11-12 playground atomic-collisions): same-word lanes serialize at ~0.86 ns (int) / 2.5 ns (float) per lane-op GPU-wide, which R16's model did not have. Blender Cycles (monkey 1024^2 64spp, GPU total) 1384 -> 1254 ms (-9.5%: shade_background -26%, intersect_closest -12%), image within noise; PTX-formalization unchanged (1791 / 172). R16 (uniform-only, CodeGenPrepare) stays off. lit atomic-aggregate.ll. | adopted | float sums round differently from any serial order (only without --strict-fp) |

Each core-only revert (not the threads', not loads first) records its first instruction, end and its free order's halves; at the function's end, with the most GPR16 pressure of any block as scheduled, a region whose free order's groups are no fewer than that leaves is entered again and scheduled freely (its input the same original order, so the same free order), the bound raised to its halves for the next. where_rows' first region (free 34 halves beside a block of 52): tput 375.4 -> 339.7 (-9.5%), row_norm_apply 854.2 -> 848.5 (-0.7%), cosh_f64 -0.2%, sincos_rows -0.1%, expm1_f64 -0.05%, tanh_f64 +0.3%, sinh_f64 +0.03%; log_dyn / logsoftmax / softmax_dyn keep E799's. agx-bench sgemm_reg4x4 2263.1 -> 2270.2 (+0.3%, 50 -> 52 GPRs, the same 32 groups), attn_decode +0.06%. Compile time: `zoc` (debug build) over the 40 largest PTX tests 258.7 s direct vs 265.1 s through AIR (+2.5%);
`agx-raise-to-air` itself ~1.6 ms per module, the AGX side of the AIR (`agx-lower-air` + codegen) no slower than
direct.

## Measured: IEEE binary32 in the default mode (`-mattr=+ieee-fp32`)

PTX keeps f32 subnormals without `.ftz` (sm_20+) and rounds `div.rn.f32` / `sqrt.rn.f32` correctly; the AGX f32 ALU
flushes subnormals and its division and square root are approximations. The PTX front end's default mode follows the hardware;
`zoc --strict-fp` (the `ieee-fp32` feature, through `air.ieee-fp32` on the AIR route) follows PTX. The suite costed
with `llc -mattr=+ieee-fp32` against the default (G14, `agx_cost`): `tput` 4.8x the default's (geometric mean over
36 kernels; 10 unchanged, without affected f32 arithmetic), up to 42x (`sgemm_reg4x4`; `layernorm_f16` 39x,
`reduce_shfl` 35x, `saxpy` 19.6x). Split with `-agx-ieee-fp32-no-denormals` (ieee-fp32 with every function's
float denormals flushed): correctly rounded division and square root alone 1.22x (7 kernels change, up to 5.05x),
denormals the rest, 3.95x. Kept as the opt-in strict mode, not the default.

Policy (the user's): anything that costs performance goes into strict floating point (`zoc --strict-fp`, the
`ieee-fp32` feature), the default stays the hardware's. So the cos reduction that meets PTX's 2^-20.4 on
[-2pi, 2pi] (a compare and select) is ieee-fp32 only, and sin(-0) = -0 comes from AGXStrictFP's small-argument
path, not from a select in the default lowering (llvm/test/CodeGen/AGX/trig-strict.ll).

## Research sources (policy)

Experiments are based only on publicly published work: open-access papers (arXiv, ACM / IEEE papers with public
copies, university repositories), open-source compilers (LLVM, Mesa) and public documentation. **Patent documents
are not used as sources**: search results that are patents or patent applications are skipped and nothing is
implemented from them. This is not a freedom-to-operate review: a published technique can still be covered by a
patent, which only a legal review can establish. Techniques already shipped in open-source compilers are preferred.

| Round | Source | Kind | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Saumya, Sundararajah, Kulkarni, "DARM: Control-Flow Melding for SIMT Thread Divergence Reduction", CGO 2022 (arXiv 2107.05681) | open-access paper | R7 |
| 1 | Lee et al., "Convergence and scalarization for data-parallel architectures", CGO 2013 | paper (public copy, Berkeley) | R1-E6 (deferred) |
| 1 | LLVM AMDGPU GCNSchedStrategy, UniformityAnalysis, SpeculativeExecution | open-source (Apache-2.0 with LLVM exception) | R11, R13, tput metric |
| 1 | Magni, Dubach, O'Boyle, thread coarsening studies (Edinburgh) | papers (university repository) | R1-E6 (deferred) |
| 1 | Lee et al., many-thread-aware prefetching for GPGPUs, MICRO 2010; NVIDIA developer blog on prefetching | paper, public blog | R12 |
| 1 | "Characterizing Warp Divergence from Pascal to Blackwell" (arXiv); NVIDIA Research, SPMD divergence management (2014) | open-access papers | R11 |
| 2 | Osama, Merrill, Cecka, Garland, "Stream-K", PPoPP 2023 (arXiv 2301.03598) | open-access paper (motivation only; the implementation is plain split-K as in open-source BLAS libraries) | A12 |
| 1 | Tillet, Kung, Cox, Triton (MAPL 2019); ML-Triton (arXiv 2503.14985) | open-access papers | background |

## Compile time (policy)

Every adopted change records its `llc` compile-time cost. A change that makes a real difference to the generated
code but costs significantly more compile time (over ~10% of `llc` time on the kernel sets, or more than a few
seconds on a single large kernel) is not enabled by default: it gets its own `llc` option, listed here, and
`zoc` and its runtime pass it only on request. So far none qualifies:

| Change | llc time | Default |
|---|---|---|
| A3 post-RA MachineScheduler | +2% (4.45 -> 4.55 s over 209 files) | on |
| A1 / A2 memory clustering | not measurable | on |
| A15 uniform constants | within noise (3.28-3.41 s vs 3.41-3.45 s over the agx_libs kernels) | off in llc, on in the PTX front end |
| A18-A21 (store-vector pressure, vector access shapes, shared divisors, 32-bit counters) | within noise (4.35 / 4.61 s on vs 4.42 / 4.58 s off over the agx_libs kernels, two runs) | on |

## Model gaps that decide the entries above

1. Occupancy (`tput`): resident groups = min(96, 2 * floor(3328 / (2 * register halves))), whole workgroups,
   64 KiB threadgroup memory per core. A1-A3 and R1, R9 depend on it.
2. Memory after ALU (A1, A2): `mem_quiet_device_store_ref` 33.6, `mem_quiet_load_ref` 20. Does a `wait` or a
   load between stores reset the stall? Do 8-16 back-to-back loads issue as modelled?
3. G13 numbers reuse G14C rows; G13 tile GEMMs (matrix ops emulated, 128 GPRs, spills) move +-20% with any
   scheduling change.
4. Launch overhead, coalescing and cache behaviour are not in `cycles` / `tput`.
5. Uniform registers (A15): the model charges nothing for a uniform source or for more preloaded uniforms. Does a
   larger uniform count slow the dispatch, or limit resident workgroups?

AGX-Performance-Simulator `tasks/ptx-frontend-agx-followups.md` lists the same gaps from the simulator's side.
